# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-11-05_TotalExtraction_42202007.pdf
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
November 18, 2024 
SUBJECT: 
Consideration of Bid #S-7-25 for an Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and 
Fencetrak Inc. d/b/a Full Tilt Services for the Belvoir Ranch Trailhead and Access 
Roads project 
______________________________________________ 
Bid #S-7-25 was solicited for the Belvoir Ranch Trailhead and Access Roads Project (Project Vicinity 
Map Included). 
 
Five (5) bidders responded to this bid, and the bid tabulation is attached.  The Bids were received 
from: 
1. Fencetrak Inc d/b/a Full Tilt Services 
2. TC Solutions 
3. Reiman Corp 
4. Resource Construction LLC 
5. Rocky Mountain Sand and Gravel  
 
The bids were reviewed by Erin Gates (Engineering), and Dustin Humphreys (1% Construction) and 
their recommendation is to accept the lowest responsive bid from Fencetrak Inc d/b/a Full Tilt 
Services. 
 
The total Bid Award is $672,507.50. 
 
Project Completion is scheduled for June 30, 2025. 
 
Funding:  
025-21-2111-40-46552 (Wyoming Outdoor Recreation Grant) 
$486,736.00 (Grant Funds) 
015-21-2111-45-21301 (Belvoir Ranch Recreation Fund) 
$121,684.00 (Cash Match) 
015-21-2111-45-21301 (Belvoir Ranch Recreation Fund) 
$64,087.50 (Amount over Cash Match) 
 

 

 

 
City of Cheyenne 
Purchasing 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
2101 O'Neil Ave., Room 309, Cheyenne, WY 82001

CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING 
AGREEMENT 
 
Bid No. S-7-25 
 
Contract No. _______ 
 
THIS AGREEMENT, entered into this ____ day of ___________, 2024, by and between 
the CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, hereinafter referred to as the “CITY”, and Fencetrak 
Inc. d/b/a Full Tilt Services, hereinafter referred to as the “CONTRACTOR”. 
 
WITNESSETH that the Contractor and the City, for the considerations stated herein, 
mutually agree as follows: 
 
ARTICLE 1.  STATEMENT OF WORK.  The Contractor shall furnish all supervision, 
technical personnel, labor, materials, machinery, tools, equipment, and services, including utility 
and transportation services, and perform and complete all work in an efficient and workmanlike 
manner in the construction of the "Belvoir Ranch Trailhead and Access Roads" project all in 
strict accordance with the Contract Documents including all addenda thereto, numbered and dated: 
Addendum One (1), dated October 30, 2024, Addendum Two (2), dated November 7, 2024, 
and Addendum Three (3), dated November 13, 2024. 
 
ARTICLE 2.  RESPONSIBLE DESIGNEE FOR THE CITY.  The Contractor shall in any 
and all matters relating to the scope of services to be provided under this Contract or any other 
provisions herein, contact the City Engineer, or his/her designated representative. 
 
ARTICLE 3.  TIME FRAME FOR COMPLETION.  The services to be performed under 
this Agreement shall commence on the date stipulated in the “Notice to Proceed” that will be issued 
by the City.  The work shall be completed by June 30, 2025.  If the work has not been completed 
within the time stipulated above, including any extensions of time issued by the City for excusable 
delays, the Contractor and his/her sureties shall pay the City fixed, agreed liquidated damages, as 
stipulated in the Supplemental Conditions, for each calendar day of delay until the work is 
completed. 
 
ARTICLE 4.  COMPENSATION AND METHOD OF PAYMENT.  The CITY will pay 
the CONTRACTOR for the performance of the Contract in current funds, in an amount of Six 
Hundred Seventy-Two Thousand, Five Hundred Seven Dollars, and Fifty Cents 
($672,507.50). In the event there are changes in the estimated quantities shown on the Bid 
Proposal, the unit prices multiplied by the actual quantities shall govern, and the total contract 
amount will be adjusted accordingly.  The City agrees to pay the above amount for contractual 
services in the following manner, upon receipt of appropriate documentation: 
 
a. 
The Contractor will be paid on a monthly basis for the percentage of 
estimated work completed.  Submittal will be at least seven (7) business days prior to the 
payable due date as established annually by the City Treasurer’s Office.  The pay request 
shall be submitted on the Contract Payment Request Form and Itemized Pay Request or 
9:56 am, Nov 18 2024

Page 2 of 2 
the AIA Documents G702 and G703.  The engineer will review the estimate for approval 
prior to payment. 
b. 
The City will withhold five percent (5%) of the dollar value of the work 
completed for a minimum of forty-one (41) calendar days after Notice of Final Settlement 
has been published in accordance with Wyo. Stat. §16-6-116.  Upon completion of the 
work under this Contract, the Contractor shall submit a Contractor’s Certificate of 
Completion; the Consent of Surety; Final Waivers of Lien from the Contractor, and all 
Sub-Contractors, Suppliers and Materialmen; Affidavit of Release of Liens; Affidavit of 
Payment; and a current Workers Compensation Certificate.  Final payment will not be 
made until the above documents have been received by the City and all items on the Punch 
List have been completed, and the advertising requirements have been met. 
 
ARTICLE 5.  CONTRACT.  The executed Contract Documents shall consist of the 
following: 
 
1. This Agreement;  
2. Addenda; 
3. Invitation for Bids; 
4. Instructions to Bidders; 
5. Signed Bid Proposal 
6. Supplemental Federal Provisions & 
Clauses; 
 
7. General Conditions and Insurance; 
8. Supplemental Conditions; 
9. Part IV Forms & Notices; 
10. Specifications and Special 
Provisions; and  
11. Drawings. 
 
This Agreement, together with other documents enumerated in this Article 5, which said 
other documents are as fully a part of the Contract as if hereto attached or herein repeated, forms 
the Contract between the parties hereto. 
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, THAT the governing body of the City of Cheyenne has 
authorized the Mayor as Executive Officer of the City to enter into this Agreement, and that the 
parties hereto have caused this Agreement to be executed on the day and year in the first part herein 
written. 
 
ATTEST: 
 
 
 
 
 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING 
 
____________________________  
 
______________________________ 
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk  
 
 
Patrick Collins, Mayor

Fencetrak Inc. d/b/a Full Tilt Services

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-09-03_TotalExtraction_41517613.pdf
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
September 9, 2024  
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: COUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich (via Zoom), Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Richard Johnson, 
Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff White. Absent: Patrick 
Collins, Mayor. Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk. The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda. Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support received 
by Mr. Cook and Mr. Roybal, item #20 (c) was removed from the Consent Agenda. Mr. Roybal 
moved to approve remaining items, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on August 26, 2024.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
President Esquibel opened a Public Hearing for a transfer of ownership of retail liquor license 
application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for 1734 Partners, LLC d/b/a 1734 Partners, 1734 
Meadowland Dr., Cheyenne, WY (license currently held by Old Chicago Taproom II, LLC d/b/a/ 
Old Chicago #74). Public comment was made by: John Johnson, principle 1734 partner. 
Comments were made by the following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Johnson 
and Dr. Rinne. There being no further comment or input and the purpose of the hearing having 
been met, President Esquibel closed the hearing and referred the application to Finance Committee. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located west of Converse Avenue and north of Dell Range Boulevard. Reporting for Public 
Services Committee, Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. 
Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#4583) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne establishing the zoning classification of AG Agricultural for land annexed to the City 
of Cheyenne located west of Converse Avenue and north of Dell Range Boulevard.  Mr. Roybal 
moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#4584) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from AG Agricultural to NR-3 Neighborhood 
Residential - High Density for land located west of Converse Avenue and north of Dell Range 

Boulevard.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#4585) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located east of US-30 and north of and adjacent to East Pershing Boulevard. Mr. Roybal moved to 
approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present. (#4586) 
 
            ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne establishing the zoning classification of MUR Mixed-Use Residential for land annexed 
to the City of Cheyenne located east of US-30 and north of and adjacent to East Pershing 
Boulevard. Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#4587) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from LI Light Industrial to CB Community Business 
for land located northeast of the intersection of Hutchins Drive and Industry Drive. Mr. Roybal 
moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#4588) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending Section 2.76.040, Designation of Downtown 
Development District, of Chapter 2.76, Downtown Development Authority, of Title 2, 
Administration and Personnel, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, to 
expand the Downtown Development District. Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final 
reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave. Mr. Roybal moved to amend by substitute maps provided in 
staff memo dated August 27, 2024, seconded by Mr. Segrave. Motion to amend by substitute maps 
carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Dr. 
Aldrich voting “no”. Main motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
present with the exception of Dr. Aldrich voting “no”. Comments were made by the following 
members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Laybourn. (#4589) 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) of the 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, to create BP Business Park Zone District and making conforming 
amendments to locations throughout the UDC referencing zone districts; and amending the UDC 
to correct references associated with the SS Support Services District – referred to Public Services 
Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Re-appropriating the Fiscal Year 2024 Budget of the City of Cheyenne 
according to the provisions of W.S. §§ 16-4-112 & 16-4-113. Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried. (#6423) 
 
 
RESOLUTION – Approving sanitary sewer service, by Outside User Agreement, for Oh 
Hail No LLC, for land located south of Robert Avenue and west of South Greeley Highway. Mr. 
Roybal moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Segrave. Mr. Roybal moved to amend by substitute dated 
August 30, 2024, seconded by Mr. Segrave. Motion to amend by substitute carried. Voting “yes” 
– all members of the governing body present. Main motion as amended by substitute carried. 
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Laybourn 

voting “no”. Public comment was made by: Brad Emmons, AVI, agent for applicant. Comments 
were made by the following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Laybourn, Dr. 
Rinne, Mr. Segrave, and Mr. White. (#6424) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Scenic 
Development, 5th Filing, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located west of Converse 
Avenue and north of Dell Range Boulevard) – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – 
Mr. Roybal) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for 
Andiamo Subdivision Second Filing, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located 
southeast of the intersection of West 19th Street and O’Neil Avenue) – referred to Public Services 
Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Granting an exception to the ‘201 Agreement’ for the owner of the 
property located at 4614 East Pershing Boulevard to allow installation of a small wastewater 
system (septic system) – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal)  
 
 
Contract between the City of Cheyenne Police Department and Tyler Technologies for 
maintenance and support services of the Police Department’s records management software 
(General Fund). Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to approve, seconded by Mr. 
Segrave. Motion carried in an amount not to exceed $735,215.00. Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body present. Comments were made by the following member of the governing 
body: Mr. Johnson. (#8044) 
 
[CA] 
License Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the United States of America 
Western Area Power Administration (WAPA) for the construction of the Terry Ranch Road-
Cheyenne 230-kV and the Owl Creek-Cheyenne 115kV transmission lines. Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried. (#8045) 
 
 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Ayres Associates for 
the 19th Street Drainage and Intersection Improvements Project (2019-2022 Optional 1% Sales 
Tax Fund). Mr. White moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Segrave. Motion carried in an amount 
not to exceed $712,446.00. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. Comments 
were made by the following member of the governing body: Mr. Laybourn. (#8046) 
 
 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and The Design Studio, 
Inc. (TDSi), for the restoration and remodel of the Cheyenne Pumphouse (General Fund Reserves). 
Mr. White moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Segrave. Motion carried in an amount not to exceed 
$424,200.00. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. Comments were made 
by the following members of the governing body: Mr. Johnson and Mr. Laybourn. (#8047) 
 
[CA] 
Drainage Easement Agreement granted by Kenrich, LLC to the City of Cheyenne to ensure 
maintenance of a storm water conveyance system along the Christensen Road corridor (2017 
Specific Purpose Option Tax Fund). Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried in an amount not to 
exceed $424.00. (#8048) 
 

[CA] 
Interagency Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the State of Wyoming, 
Department of State Parks and Cultural Resources, to outline projects, responsibilities, and special 
provisions at the High Plains Research Station and Arboretum (Revenue to City). Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. (#8049) 
 
[CA] 
Contract Modification #1 to Contract #7960 between the City of Cheyenne and Asphalt 
Preservation LLC, for additional materials required to complete the 2024 Surface Seal #1 Project. 
(2023-2026 Optional 1% Sales Tax Fund) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 [CA] 
Contract Modification #1 to Contract #7968 between the City of Cheyenne and Asphalt 
Preservation LLC, for additional materials required to complete the 2024 Surface Seal #2 Project. 
(2021 Specific Purpose Option Tax Fund) – referred to Finance Committee. 
  
 
Consideration of Bid #S-2-25 for an Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Reiman 
Corp. for the Depot Renovation Project (2021 Specific Purpose Option Tax Fund). Mr. White 
declared a conflict of interest and vacated council chambers. Mr. Johnson moved to approve, 
seconded by Dr. Rinne. Motion carried in an amount not to exceed $1,426,265.00. Voting “yes” – 
all members of the governing body present in council chambers. Comments were made by the 
following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Segrave. (#8050) 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #S-4-25 for an Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and S&S 
Builders LLC, for the Dutcher Athletic Complex Restroom and Concession Building Project. 
(2021 Specific Purpose Option Tax Fund, Lottery Proceeds and Recreation Mill Grant) – referred 
to Finance Committee.  
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #S-24-24 for an Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Traffic 
Safety Services, Inc., for the 2024 Thermoplastic Markings #1 Project. (2019-2022 Optional 1% 
Sales Tax Fund) – referred to Finance Committee.  
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-1-25 for 2 greens mowers and 3 tee box mowers for the Golf 
Division. (Golf Fund) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #S-1-25 for an Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Orion 
Environmental Inc., for the Cheyenne Railcar Project, Bid Package No. 1, Asbestos and Lead Paint 
Abatement Project. (General Fund Reserves and Economic Development Administration Grant) – 
referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 
 
No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 
Announcement was made that a Calcutta benefitting the Wyoming Breast Cancer Initiative, 
will be held October 4 – October 6, 2024 at Peppermill Bar, 1618 Stillwater Ave., Cheyenne, WY 
(10% of proceeds go to the charity). 
 
The following members of the governing body made comments under Other Business: Dr. 
Aldrich, Mr. Johnson and Mr. White. 
 
 

 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:42 p.m. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Kristina F. Jones, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
City Clerk 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: 
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
 
 
September 13, 2024 
 
 
 


--- END OF FILE ---


# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-09-03_TotalExtraction_37470466.pdf
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Seth Lloyd, Senior Planner 
Subject: PLTA-24-6 – Amending UDC to create the BP zone district, make conforming amendments 
to the code, and to amend sections which reference zone districts. 
Date: 
September 9, 2024 
 
Background:  
The item for consideration is a UDC Text Amendment. This is approved by Ordinance.    
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral  
9/9/2024 
• 
Public Services Committee  
9/17/2024 
• 
Second reading  
 
9/23/2024 
• 
Public Services Committee  
10/8/2024 
• 
Third and final reading   
10/14/2024 
 
Planning Commission Recommendation:  
The Planning Commission heard this item at their September 3, 2024, meeting and unanimously voted 
to recommend the Governing Body approve the proposed text amendment. 
 
The Planning Commission Staff report is attached and has not been changed, except that the redline 
Ordinance attached at the end was removed to reduce confusion. The Ordinance in this packet is not 
substantively changed from the Ordinance reviewed by the Planning Commission. 
 
Attachments: 
1. Ordinance 
2. Redlined Ordinance 
3. Planning Commission Staff Report 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

 
ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE UNIFIED DEVELOPMENT CODE 
(UDC) OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, TO CREATE THE BP 
BUSINESS PARK ZONE DISTRICT AND MAKING CONFORMING 
AMENDMENTS 
TO 
LOCATIONS 
THROUGHOUT 
THE 
UDC 
REFERENCING ZONE DISTRICTS; AND AMENDING THE UDC TO 
CORRECT REFERENCES ASSOCIATED WITH THE SS SUPPORT 
SERVICES DISTRICT." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the text amendment 
that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.4.1.d of the UDC. 
 
 
Section 2.  That the intent of this text amendment is to provide flexibility for and encourage 
a variety of uses in large-scale business parks, and to update various locations where zones are 
referenced to include newly created zone districts. 
 
 
Section 3.  That on September 3, 2024, the Planning Commission conducted a public 
hearing, reviewed the proposed text amendment, and recommended approval of said text 
amendment. 
 
Section 4.  That Sub-section 1.4.3, Defined Terms, in Section 1.4 Interpretation, of Article 
1, General Provisions is amended to create a new definition "Caretaker Unit. One or more living 
quarters or lodging facilities located within a non-residential building for the use of employees of 
the use. A Caretaker Unit shall not be considered a 'residential use' or 'residential unit' for the 
purposes of this code but shall be considered part of the non-residential square footage for the 
purposes of the code. The combined square footage of all Caretaker Units shall not comprise more 
than ten (10) percent of the principal use's building square footage (should dwelling area exceed 
this amount, the living area shall be determined to be a "Mixed-use Dwelling" and a residential 
use for the purposes of this code). A Caretaker Unit is allowed as part of the approval of the 
principal use without any more restrictive approval process than that warranted for the principal 
use." 
 
 
Section 5.  That Sub-section 5.1.2, Zoning Districts Established, in Section 5.1 General

Section 7.  That Sub-section 5.4.7 BP – Business Park, of Section 5.4 Commercial and 
Mixed-use Districts Standards of Article 5, Zoning Regulations, is created to read as follows:  
5.4.7 BP-Business Park 
 
a. 
Intent.  The Business Park (BP) district is intended to support small and large-scale 
industrial development while providing essential commercial services along 
regional transportation routes in an industrial park setting. The district integrates 
manufacturing, processing, research and development, and shipping activities in 

 
addition to retail, dining, and convenience stores to enhance functionality and 
appeal for businesses and employees. 
 
 
b. 
Applicability.  The BP district is applicable to areas on the edge of the community 
that are intended to support large-scale industrial, manufacturing, shipping, 
research and development, and data center uses. The district is typically found close 
to major transportation routes, making commercial uses appropriate. The district 
shall contain not less than 320 acres of land (interrupted only by roads, railroads, 
highways and natural features (such as ridges and waterways). Specifically, large-
scale areas within the future land use designations in the comprehensive plan of 
Community Business, Mixed-Use Employment, and Industrial – including 
Community/Regional 
Activity 
Centers. 
Areas 
currently 
designated 
Agricultural/Rural (either inside or outside of the Urban Service Boundary) may 
also be appropriate for this zone district if near to regional transportation routes and 
if development of the area extends appropriate infrastructure.  
 
 
c. 
Context.  This district is most appropriate in areas with large acreage and access to 
major regional transportation infrastructure. 
 
 
d. 
Lot and Building Standards.  The following lot and building standards shall 
apply: 
 
TYPE BP: Business Park District Lot and Building Standards 
Lot Area 
n/a 
Lot Frontage 
n/a 
Setbacks (Minimum) 
Front 
All buildings shall be set 
back 25’ from all property 
lines adjacent to streets; 
10 feet from all other 
internal property lines; 
and 25 feet from all 
property lines shared with 
another zone district. 
Side 
Rear 
Building Height 
n/a 
Maximum Coverage 
90% 
 
 
e. 
Permitted Uses.  The uses permitted in the BP district are specified in Table 5-1 as 
either "permitted" or "administrative" or "conditional." 
 
 
f. 
Site and Building Design Standards.  Due to the nature of development and 
context of this zone district, the following development standards shall apply to 
development in the BP zone.  
 
 
1. 
Parking, Lot Access, and Circulation.  The minimum required 
Automobile Parking standards of UDC 6.2.4.a shall not apply to property 

 
within the BP district. 
 
 
2.  
Landscaping.  Development shall comply with the standards of UDC 6.3, 
Landscaping Standards, with the following modifications: 
 
 
 
(a) 
Tree Requirement.  Instead of requiring tree points (UDC 6.3.4.a), 
every development shall plant one (1) tree for everyone hundred 
(100) feet of street frontage. Trees shall be planted within 100’ of a 
right-of-way, within 15’ of a parking lot, or within 20’ of a building. 
 
 
 
(b)  
Shrub Substitution.  In lieu of planting trees, a developer may opt 
to plant six (6) shrubs as a substitute for each required tree. 
 
 
 
(c) 
Street Trees.  The Street Tree requirements of UDC 6.3.6 shall not 
apply. 
 
 
 
(d) 
Common Landscape Plan.  The submittal of a Common 
Landscape Plan and the standards within UDC 6.3.10 shall not 
apply.  
 
 
3. 
Building Design Standards.  Any development not in the “Industrial 
Uses” category in Section 5.1.4, Table 5-1 shall comply with the 
design standards of UDC 6.9 (or UDC 6.8 if meeting the 
applicability of UDC 6.8).  
 
 
Section 8.  That Sub-section UDC 5.8.6 Outdoor Storage, of Section 5.8 Supplemental 
Provisions, of Article 5 Zoning Regulations is amended to read as follows: "Where Outdoor 
Storage is allowed, the storage area shall be screened from all adjacent streets with a Level 3 buffer 
as described in Section 6.3.5, Table 6-11. Where Outdoor Storage is allowed in the BP zone 
district, the storage area shall be screened from all adjacent streets with a 6’ wall or solid fence (a 
landscape buffer is not required)." 
 
 
Section 9.  That Sub-section UDC 6.2.2.a General Applicability, of Sub-section 6.2.2 
Applicability, of Section 6.2 Parking, Lot Access and Circulation, of Article 6 Design Regulations 
is amended to read as follows: "a. General Applicability. The standards in this section shall apply 
to all new construction and changes of use in all zoning districts, except that no parking is required 
in the CBD, SS, or BP districts or the UU Overlay District. When sites do elect to provide parking 
in the CBD, SS, or BP districts or the UU Overlay District, all maximum quantity, location, design 
standards and specific limitations in this Article shall apply." 
 
 
Section 10.  That Sub-section UDC 6.3.5.a Required Buffer Level of Sub-section 6.3.5 
Screening and Buffering, of Section 6.3 Landscape Standards, of Article 6 Design Regulations is 
amended add BP and SS to Table 6-10 as follows:

Section 11.  That sub-section UDC 6.5.4.b, Mixed-Use, Commercial and Industrial Zoning 
Districts, of Sub-section 6.5.4 Sign Allowances, of Section 6.5 Signs, of Article 6 Design 
Regulations is amended to read as follows: "The allowances in Table 6-16 apply to the NB, CB, 
CBD, MUR, MUB, MUE, LI, HI, AD, P, SS, BP, and PUD districts."  
 
 
Section 12.  That sub-section UDC 6.5.4.b, Mixed-Use, Commercial and Industrial Zoning 
Districts, of Sub-section 6.5.4 Sign Allowances, of Section 6.5 Signs, of Article 6 Design 
Regulations is amended to change the language for neighborhood identification signs as follows: 
 "Up to 2 per each street intersection with an arterial street and no more than 32 square feet 
for each sign. In industrial business parks in the MUE, BP, and HI zone districts, the 
allowance for neighborhood identification signs may be up to 200 square feet per sign. The 
area allowance for each arterial intersection may be allocated to more frequent signs at 
smaller intersections within the neighborhood." 
 "No sign or structure supporting the sign shall exceed a height of 6'. In industrial business 
parks in the MUE, BP, and HI zone districts, sign structures may be up to 20' high, and 
neighborhood identification signs may be placed on prominent structures having a non-
sign primary function (such as a water tower, cell tower, electrical facility, or similar)." 
 
 
Section 13.  That sub-section UDC 6.5.4.c.3, of Subsection 6.5.4.c Electronic Message 
Centers, of Sub-section 6.5.4 Sign Allowances, of Section 6.5 Signs, of Article 6 Design 
Regulations is amended to read as follows: "Electronic Message Centers may be allowed in the 
CB, MUB, CBD, P, AD, SS, BP, LI, and HI Districts, subject to the following:" 
 

 
 
Section 14.  That sub-section UDC 6.5.7.h, of Subsection 6.5.7 Prohibited Signs, of 
Section 6.5 Signs, of Article 6 Design Regulations, is amended to read as follows: "h. Animated 
signs, except in the CB, BP, LI, or HI districts." 
 
 
Section 15.  That sub-section UDC 6.5.8.a, of Subsection 6.5.8 Billboards, of Section 6.5 
Signs, of Article 6 Design Regulations is amended to read as follows: "a. Billboards shall be 
permitted only in the MUB, CB, BP, LI, and HI zoning districts, except as provided in subsections 
(d) or (e)." 
 
 
Section 16.  That Sub-section 6.9.2, Applicability of Section 6.9 General Commercial 
Design Standards, of Article 6 Design Regulations is amended to read as follows: 
 
These standards apply to community/regional activity centers, commercial buildings, or 
other similar areas where commercial buildings, with automobile orientation, can impact 
the community negatively unless designed in a coordinated manner. They apply in addition 
to any generally applicable design requirements of this Article, and the standards of 
Articles 4 and 5. Specifically, they apply to new construction and additions to buildings in 
the following zone districts: 
 
 
 
a. 
CB 
 
 
b. 
SS 
 
 
c. 
BP, for non-industrial development 
 
 
Section 17.  That the aforementioned sections of the UDC are hereby amended as set out 
within this ordinance and sections currently existing within the UDC not set out within this 
ordinance remain unchanged. 
 
 
Section 18.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable.  If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 19.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and 
publication.

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE UNIFIED DEVELOPMENT CODE 
(UDC) OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, TO CREATE THE BP 
BUSINESS PARK ZONE DISTRICT AND MAKING CONFORMING 
AMENDMENTS 
TO 
LOCATIONS 
THROUGHOUT 
THE 
UDC 
REFERENCING ZONE DISTRICTS; AND AMENDING THE UDC TO 
CORRECT REFERENCES ASSOCIATED WITH THE SS SUPPORT 
SERVICES DISTRICT." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the text amendment 
that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.4.1.d of the UDC. 
 
 
Section 2.  That the intent of this text amendment is to provide flexibility for and encourage 
a variety of uses in large-scale business parks, and to update various locations where zones are 
referenced to include newly created zone districts. 
 
 
Section 3.  That on September 3, 2024, the Planning Commission conducted a public 
hearing, reviewed the proposed text amendment, and recommended approval of said text 
amendment. 
 
Section 4.  That Sub-section 1.4.3, Defined Terms, in Section 1.4 Interpretation, of Article 
1, General Provisions is amended to create a new definition "Caretaker Unit. One or more living 
quarters or lodging facilities located within a non-residential building for the use of employees of 
the use. A Caretaker Unit shall not be considered a 'residential use' or 'residential unit' for the 
purposes of this code but shall be considered part of the non-residential square footage for the 
purposes of the code. The combined square footage of all Caretaker Units shall not comprise more 
than ten (10) percent of the principal use's building square footage (should dwelling area exceed 
this amount, the living area shall be determined to be a "Mixed-use Dwelling" and a residential 
use for the purposes of this code). A Caretaker Unit is allowed as part of the approval of the 
principal use without any more restrictive approval process than that warranted for the principal 
use." 
 
 
Section 5.  That Sub-section 5.1.2, Zoning Districts Established, in Section 5.1 General 
Provisions, of Article 5, Zoning Regulations is amended to create a new row "BP | Business Park" 
after the "MUE | Mixed-Use Employment Emphasis∞ row, and a new row "SS | Support Services" 
after the "AD | Airport District" row.

of Section 5.1 General Provisions, of Article 5, Zoning Regulations is amended to create a new 
column “BP” between columns "MUE" and "AD" showing uses as follows:

severable.  If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 19.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and 
publication.

PLTA-24-6 
 
 
September 3, 2024 
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   (Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
PLANNING COMMISSION  
SEPTEMBER 3, 2024 
STAFF REPORT  
 
REQUEST: 
 
A Text Amendment amending the Unified Development Code to create a new zone 
district “BP Business Park” – to include modification to multiple sections of code to 
include references to “BP” where applicable. Also to correct the code to include 
references to “SS” where applicable. 
 
CASE NUMBER:  
PLTA-24-6 
PREPARED BY: 
Seth Lloyd, Senior Planner, Planning and Development Department 
 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION:  
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve text amendments to the Unified Development Code 
to create the BP Business Park zone district and amending references to zoning districts by creating 
Section 5.4.7 and amending Sections 1.4.3, 5.1.2. 5.1.4 Table 5-1, 5.8.6, 6.2.2.a, 6.3.5.a, 6.5.4.b, 6.5.4.c, 
6.5.7.h, 6.5.8.a, and 6.9.2 as outlined in this staff report and recommended by staff, noting the requests 
meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.   
 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The City’s Unified Development Code (UDC) was adopted by the Governing Body on January 23, 2012, by 
Ordinance Number 3943 with an effective date of April 30, 2012.  The Unified Development Code is the 
regulatory document carrying out the vision of PlanCheyenne and is reviewed annually pursuant to UDC 
1.1.7 [Annual Review Requirement] to determine if any particular standard or regulation is timely, 
relevant and adequately addresses current market conditions. In addition to the annual review, the 
Planning and Development Director and City Engineer are authorized under UDC 2.4.1.b.3 to bring 
forward text amendments on their own initiative.  
 
The proposed text amendments are to create a new zone district “BP Business Park” in the UDC. The 
impetus behind the proposed text amendment is to create a zone district which closely matches the 
current County PUD for North Range Business Park. North Range Business Park is outside of City limits but 
uses City water and sewer with an Outside User’s Agreement. The Outside User’s Agreement requires the 
business park to annex once it becomes eligible (adjacent to City limits), and the park was made adjacent 
to City limits by a separate annexation earlier in 2024.  
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PLTA-24-6 
 
 
September 3, 2024 
The BP zone is a combination of light industrial and community business zoning, allowing a mix of 
industrial, service, retail, and office uses. All but the most intense of non-residential uses are allowed. The 
business parks located on the edges of the community are often located along regional transportation 
routes. This location is attractive to regional-serving commercial uses, along with industrial uses. The 
proposed zone district was created with input from Cheyenne Leads on what types of businesses are 
locating in business parks. 
 
There are areas of the City where parcels flip back and forth between commercial and industrial zoning 
depending on the specific project proposed. There is even a recent example of this occurring on a zone 
change request that came to planning commission in the last month. The BP zone could not only apply to 
North Range Business Park, but also other existing business parks in the City.    
 
With the creation of a new zone district, multiple sections of code referencing zone districts by name also 
need updating. When the SS zone was established, not all of these sections were updated, so while these 
sections are opened to include the BP zone, the SS zone is being added at the same time. 
 
The scale and location of business parks warrants some special consideration for design standards. The 
code amendment has BP adopt the design standards of 6.9 for non-industrial development, and gives 
some special design standard considerations for parking, landscaping, and neighborhood identification 
signage. A universally-applicable definition was added for ‘caretaker unit’ to give a path forward for a 
small living quarters on the same site as a industrial or commercial facility. 
 
PROPOSED AMENDMENTS: 
Attached is the complete language of the amendment in its Ordinance format. A summary of the 
amendments are as follows: 
- 
Amend 1.4.3 to create a definition for ‘caretaker unit’, allowing said units to exist on properties 
in zones which would not normally allow residential or lodging type-uses. This gives a clear path 
forward for sites which desire to provide this type of accommodation 
- 
Amend 5.1.2 to include BP and SS on the ‘established zones’ list 
- 
Amend 5.1.4 to include the BP zone’s allowed uses: 
o Permitted uses: Community fields, service businesses, entertainment, office, retail, 
automobile repair, gas stations, restaurants, data center, rail services, non-polluting 
industrial uses, limited outdoor storage, government uses, utility stations. 
o Conditional uses: mixed-use residential, assembly, schools, age-restricted entertainment, 
storage, outdoor sales, higher-intensity industrial 
- 
Create 5.4.7 for the BP zone district 
o Intent for a commercial-industrial zone near regional transportation routes 
o Dimensional standards (setbacks, coverage, etc.) 
o Minimum parking not required 
o Landscaping exception for trees (tree points and street trees) 
o Uses 6.9 (or 6.8 if applicable) design standards for non-industrial uses 
- 
Amend 5.8.6 to reduce outdoor storage screening requirements in the BP 
- 
Amend 6.2.2.a to indicate minimum parking is not required in the SS or BP zones 
- 
Amend 6.3.5.a to include the BP and SS zones on the buffering requirements 
- 
Amend various portions of 6.5 to give BP and SS similar sign allowances to the CB zone 
- 
Amend 6.5.4.b to give business parks a larger neighborhood identification signage allowance 
- 
Amend 6.9.2 to indicate the standards of 6.9 apply to non-industrial development in the BP zone 
 
 

PLTA-24-6 
 
 
September 3, 2024 
REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Review of a UDC text amendment shall be measured by the following three (3) criteria of Section 2.4.1.d: 
 
1. COMPREHENSIVE PLAN:  
“The amendment is in accordance with general goals and policies of the Comprehensive Plan, or 
is necessary to address an emerging issue not anticipated in the Comprehensive Plan and is not 
inconsistent with the plan.” 
 
This proposed text amendment is in accordance with the goals and policies of the Comprehensive 
Plan. 
 
The Comprehensive Plan has a point designation entitled “Community/Regional Activity Center”. 
This designation is often found at interstate interchanges and are expected to “serve Cheyenne 
residents as well as residents of the surrounding region”. They are expected to “typically include 
large-scale retail and employment uses, mixed-use development and residential”. 
 
The Comprehensive Plan also has a future land use designation “Mixed-Use Employment 
Campus”. This is anticipated to develop with “a variety of uses, with a primary focus on 
employment designed in a business campus setting”. While PlanCheyenne does encourage these 
areas to develop with a campus-like development (including plazas and pedestrian-friendly 
amenities), the examples given of existing Cheyenne ‘business campuses’ are really more 
‘business parks’:  Swan Ranch, North Range Business Park, and Cheyenne Business Parkway. None 
of these Cheyenne business parks have a true campus-like atmosphere like those found at places 
like the Denver Tech Center, the Anschutz Medical Campus in Aurora, or the Interlocken park in 
Broomfield.  
  
The City does have a zone which is intended to catch the Comprehensive Plan’s vision for “Mixed-
use Employment Campus”: the MUE zone. The current MUE Mixed-use Employment zone, as 
found in the zoning code, contemplates a very specific type of campus-style development. The 
type of development encouraged is small-scale industrial and commercial uses, with a significant 
office presence. Higher-intensity versions of retail, service, or industry are prohibited. Most of the 
City’s business parks are looking for higher-intensity uses, including specifically the higher-
intensity commercial uses anticipated at major interchanges and encouraged by the 
comprehensive plan’s “Community/Regional Activity Center” designations at these interchanges. 
MUE design standards for the current MUE zone are centered around a unified, campus-like 
atmosphere, and is not as applicable to the disjointed, large-distance type of development often 
found in current Cheyenne business parks. There is a place for campus-style zoning, so Staff did 
not propose removing or replacing this zone district to accommodate the intent of the BP zoning.  
 
The large swaths of “MUE” as designated in the Comprehensive Plan on the edges of the 
community could have a campus-style development, but to expect all such areas to develop with 
campus-style development is probably unrealistic. The BP zone is proposed to accommodate a 
mix of commercial and industrial uses in a business-park-style development pattern instead of a 
more compact business-campus-style development pattern.  
    
 
 
 
 

PLTA-24-6 
 
 
September 3, 2024 
2. STATE STATUTORY REQUIREMENTS:  
The amendment is consistent with the Wyoming Statutes, and specifically the Municipal Planning 
and Zoning statutes. The following Articles are all listed verbatim from Wyoming State Statute. 
 
WYO 15-1-601. 
The governing body of any city or town, by ordinance, may: 
 
(d) All regulations shall be made: 
(i) In accordance with a comprehensive plan and 
designed to: 
(A) Lessen congestion in the streets; 
(B) Secure safety from fire, panic and other 
dangers; 
(C) Promote health and general welfare; 
(D) Provide adequate light and air; 
(E) Prevent the overcrowding of land; 
(F) Avoid undue concentration of population; 
and 
(G) 
Facilitate 
adequate 
provisions 
for 
transportation, water, sewerage, schools, parks 
and other public requirements. 
 
Assessment: This amendment proposes amending the zoning code in accordance with the 
comprehensive plan and is in accordance with the purposes of state statute.  
 
3. HEALTH, SAFETY, AND PUBLIC WELFARE:  
“The amendment promotes the general health, safety and public welfare.” 
 
This amendment provides for a zone district tailored to the large business parks on the edges of 
the community. These parks provide economic benefits to the City, but the application of urban-
level codes to these suburban-style (or even rural/exurban-style) areas has created challenges for 
development. Regulations and separation of uses that may be applicable in areas with large 
quantities of dense residential development may not be as necessary when applied to the large 
distances involved with business parks.    
 
 
CONCLUSION:  
 
Staff concludes that this text amendment meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d. 
 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION:  
 
Staff recommends approval of text amendments to the Unified Development Code to create the BP 
Business Park zone district and amending references to zoning districts by creating Section 5.4.7 and 
amending Sections 1.4.3, 5.1.2. 5.1.4 Table 5-1, 5.8.6, 6.2.2.a, 6.3.5.a, 6.5.4.b, 6.5.4.c, 6.5.7.h, 6.5.8.a, and 
6.9.2 as outlined in this staff report.   
 
 
Attachments: 
1. Proposed Ordinance

At the August 5, 2024 Planning Commission meeting, the Planning Commission voted to postpone the 
Scenic Development, 5th Filing Expedited Plat due to concerns relating to BOPU easements, the 
transportation network for the area, the block size, and the pedestrian network. 
Staff has not received word on the location or provision of BOPU easements from BOPU or the 
applicant. Staff has talked with the MPO and pedestrian networks are not reviewed nor are they within 
their scope at this time (unless the complete streets concept is being evaluated for an area). Staff is still 
recommending the exception to block size due to the existing conditions of development in this area. At 
this time, Staff has not received an update to the transportation network for this area. 
Should Planning Commission still feel that an update to the transportation network is necessary for Final 
Plat approval, alternative motions could be made. The Planning Commission could approve only the 
Preliminary Plat (with conditions) – instead of the expedited plat, or the Planning Commission could 
postpone the item again. Should Planning Commission feel that the update is not needed at this time, 
the original recommendation in the staff report can be used. 
Alternative Motion options:
Option 1:
Staff recommends approval of the Preliminary Plat to the Governing Body with the following exception 
and conditions:
1.
Exception:
a.
Exception to block size.
2.
Conditions:
a.
Provide BOPU easements over any facilities requiring them, to the satisfaction of the 
BOPU.
b.
Correct the dedication for names and formatting. 
c.
Plat the existing streets adjacent to this development as shown in the current 
configuration of Connect 2045 or submit an update to Connect 2045 to reflect the 
changes in the street network for Section 20 as proposed.  
Option 2:
Staff recommends postponement of the expedited plat to the October 7th, 2024 Planning Commission 
Meeting.

RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the Scenic Development, 5th Filing Expedited Preliminary Plat, advancing the Final Plat 
to the Governing Body with staff recommended exceptions conditions. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
x

PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting approval of a preliminary plat for roughly 8.80 acres of land. The proposed plat 
would combine 3 Lots into 1 Lot for multifamily development. This plat is accompanying an annexation and a 
zoning map amendment.   
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Preliminary Plat, the Commission 
must make the following findings required in UDC 2.1.2.d  – a detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.2.d.1) 
 
On the future land use map found in PlanCheyenne, the subject land has been designated as 
Mixed-use Commercial / Urban Residential. The Mixed-Use Commercial Emphasis category 
encompasses existing and future developments, many of which align with activity centers. This 
category includes a wide variety of land uses and activities, focused primarily on commercial 
activities, but also including other employment, public, and residential uses. The Urban 
Residential designation encourages a range of more intensive residential uses, although the 
intent is not to change existing stable neighborhoods. The existing neighborhood has a mix of

PUDC-24-107 
 
 
August 5, 2024 
housing types and densities, and the proposed development fits within the range of the existing 
neighborhood, while providing a different housing option. 
 
Foundation 1 of PlanCheyenne encourages community growth. This development is in response 
to market demands and is within the Urban Service Boundary. The development patterns 
proposed by this plat are compatible with the designations found on the future land use map. 
 
Connect 2045 has a road extending northward from the Cut-off Road Rue Terre intersection 
going northward toward the west edge of the property. The Master Transportation network for 
this area is currently being reviewed by the Metropolitan Planning Organization, City Engineer’s 
Office and the property owners within the area. Once the new network is determined, Connect 
2024 will need to be updated. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.  
 
2) The application reflects sound planning and urban design principles with respect to existing 
and potential future development in the vicinity, including consideration of current and future 
streets, open spaces, blocks and lots, and public utilities or facilities. (UDC 2.1.2.d.2) 
 
The proposed lot is to allow for multifamily development similar to the development to the east. 
All multifamily buildings will be under one ownership. The plat also extends E Carlson to allow it 
to continue further west or connect into a new road.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
3) The division of land meets the purposes, intent, and all applicable standards in Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.2.d.3) 
 
Community Facility Fees (4.2): 
For fees based on acreage, the area of the plat is pro-rated to the nearest tenth of an acre. 
Lands reserved as unbuildable (for right-of-way, detention, parks, remainder lots, etc), are 
exempt from fees. 
Land Acquisition Fee  
 
 
 
$3,080 ($350 per acre) 
Infrastructure Fee  
 
 
 
$3,080 ($350 per acre) 
Total Land Acquisition & Infrastructure Fees 
$6,160 
 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, the Land Acquisition & Infrastructure fees are payable at 
the time of the signing of the final plat.  
 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, Enhancement Fees are payable at the issuance of a 
building permit for each structure. 
Residential Enhancement Fee 
$400 per dwelling unit 
 
 
 
Transportation Network (4.3): 
This plat proposes extending E Carlson to the edge of the plat. Connect 2045 has multiple 
roads running through Section 20. Connectivity is currently lacking in this area. Connect 
2045 has a road extending northward from the Cut-off Road Rue Terre intersection going 
northward toward the west edge of the property. If this alignment is the optimal alignment 
for the street network, this plat should dedicate at least a ½ street along the western edge. 
However, The Master Transportation network for this area is currently being reviewed by 

PUDC-24-107 
 
 
August 5, 2024 
the Metropolitan Planning Organization, City Engineer’s Office and the property owners 
within the area.  
 
The current block is excessively sized compared to regulations for the area. Even if a street 
were located on the edge of this development heading to the closest possible connection 
point to the south, the block perimeter would be over 2 miles in length, more than 3 times 
the maximum allowed by the UDC. Due to these existing conditions, an exception to block 
size is recommended. 
 
There is a proposal by this developer and Sam’s Club to the south to create a pedestrian 
connection from an earlier phase of this multifamily residential subdivision to the 
commercial properties to the south. If built, the connection would greatly reduce the 
perimeter for pedestrians and cyclists. 
 
Open Space (4.4): 
Open space is required at 750sqft per unit or 8% of the gross development area, whichever 
is less. Due to the nature of the proposed development pattern, open space will be taken 
care of with future development actions. 
 
Required Improvements (4.5): 
This plat appears to be able to provide all required utilities to the lot within this 
development. Easements are not provided for BOPU utilities, these easements need to be 
provided to the satisfaction of the BOPU. Easements are provided to other utilities.   
 
Public Safety Fees (4.6): 
Public Safety Fees are required at the time of construction of any residential building on the 
proposed lot. Per UDC Section 4.6.3.b, Public Safety Fees are payable at the issuance of a 
building permit for each structure.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.   
 
4) Any phasing proposed in the application is clearly indicated and demonstrates a logical and 
well-planned development pattern. This may include consideration of amenities and public 
facilities of the subdivision within each phase compared to the anticipated timing or the 
likelihood of future phases being constructed as planned. (UDC 2.1.2.d.4) 
 
No specific phasing is proposed with this project. 
 
Review criterion not applicable.   
 
5) The impacts identified in specific studies or technical reports that were prepared or requested 
to be prepared in association with the application, and whether the findings and 
recommendations of these reports, or any modifications or conditions on approval of the 
application, can mitigate these impacts. (UDC 2.1.2.d.5) 
 
Drainage: 
A drainage memo was provided showing compliance with original study for the area. There were 
no concerns with the memo provided. 
 
 

PUDC-24-107 
 
 
August 5, 2024 
Traffic: 
A waiver for the traffic study was provided. The Engineering Department has accepted the 
waiver for this Plat but has stated that future development actions will require additional TIS 
information/updates regarding Phase 2 of the development.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
  
6) Lots proposed for development shall be buildable lots according to the existing or proposed 
zoning district standards, result in design and development patterns that meet the Intent and 
Applicability statements of the existing or proposed zoning district, and not aggravate 
nonconforming setbacks or currently adopted building and fire code access and separation 
requirements. (UDC 2.1.2.d.6) 
 
The proposed application occurs in concert with a proposal to zone the land NR-3 Neighborhood 
Residential – High Density. The NR-3 zone is intended primarily for high-density, urban 
residential uses with consistent neighborhood character. The NR-3 zone allows multifamily 
development as a use by right. The proposed lot type would be MD1. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
7) All parcels not proposed as buildable lots and which are not dedicated to and accepted by the 
City shall have a clearly defined entity responsible for ongoing maintenance.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.7) 
 
All Lots are proposed to be buildable Lots.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
8) The application will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development for 
adjacent property considering the existing zoning and Comprehensive Plan policies for that 
property.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.8) 
 
The street network in this area would generally require a street within this plat boundary, which 
would be dedicated with this plat. Additions to the street network would facilitate surrounding 
development. Staffs understanding is that there are ongoing discussions with landowners to 
rearrange the street network in this area. This plat will not otherwise create any undue 
influence on existing or potential development.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.    
 
9) The application is not harmful to the public health, safety, and welfare. (UDC 2.1.2.d.9) 
 
With a resolution of the street network issue, City staff is satisfied that this proposed plat would 
not adversely affect the public health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
10) Remainder lot(s) shall be clearly identified on the preliminary plat and shall not be developed 
or proposed for development for purposes.  Remainder lot(s) shall be noted and described on 
all plats either graphically or by legal description. (UDC 2.1.2.d.10) 
 

PUDC-24-107 
 
 
August 5, 2024 
Remainder lots are not proposed with this plat. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
11) The layout and design of the final plat is in substantial compliance with the approved 
preliminary plat considering the number of lots or parcels, street and block layout, and access, 
and any deviations in the final plat brings the application in further compliance with the 
Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.3.d.1) 
 
The Final Plat submitted with the application is substantially similar to the Preliminary Plat 
submitted. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
12) The application meets any conditions or modifications of the approved preliminary plat. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.2) 
 
Through the review process and application of the Preliminary Plat, Staff recommends minor 
corrections as conditions to the plat. These corrections should be resolved prior to the final plat 
being recorded.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.   
   
13) The application meets the purposes, intent, and applicable standards of Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.3) 
 
As a concurrent Preliminary and Final Plat application, this Final Plat review criterion is covered 
by the equivalent Preliminary Plat review criterion (subsection 3 above). 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
x 
Dell Range/Section 20 Corridor Study  
 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1. 
 
The Dell Range Boulevard Corridor Study Phase II – Section 20 plan used as its starting point the 
“2040 Roadway Vision” map in the Cheyenne Area Master Transportation Plan, City of 
Cheyenne Version, adopted April 2014. This network was revisited and became the alignment 
shown in the Connect 2045 plan. The main difference was the removal of Melton Street as a 
major street. This plan still showed an anticipated north-south street near the western edge of 
this plat. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, comments were received regarding the following issues: 
1. Minor clerical corrections. 
2. Utility and easement correction. 

PUDC-24-107 
 
 
August 5, 2024 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff analysis is that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the expedited plat application as submitted by the applicant with exceptions and conditions.  
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the expedited preliminary plat, forwarding the Final Plat to the 
Governing Body. 
2. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat with conditions and that a final plat addressing the 
conditions of approval be prepared and presented to the Governing Body.  
3. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat as submitted by the applicant and forward the 
preliminary plat to the Governing Body. 
4. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat subject to staff’s recommended conditions and 
forward the preliminary plat to the Governing Body.  
5. Recommend denial of the preliminary plat based on findings of denial. The Planning Commission 
must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the motion, 
findings for denial must be stated. 
6. Postpone the project until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
The Planning Commission may modify staff’s recommended conditions, delete conditions, add 
conditions, or decide contrary to staff’s recommendation. In the event conditions are modified, the 
conditions shall relate to applicable review criteria. In the event that the Commission acts contrary to 
staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the Commission shall cite specific reasons 
(based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the expedited Preliminary Plat, advancing the Final Plat to the Governing 
Body with the following exception and conditions: 
1. Exception: 
a. Exception to block size. 
2. Prior to approval: 
a. Provide BOPU easements over any facilities requiring them, to the satisfaction of the 
BOPU. 
3. Prior to recording: 
a. Correct the dedication for names and formatting.  
b. Connect 2045 be updated to reflect the changes in the street network for Section 20.  
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Zoning Map  
2. Land Use Plan Map 
3. Master Application Form 
4. Preliminary Plat 
5. Final Plat

CITY OF CHEYENNE PLANNING COMMISSION
SEPTEMBER 3, 2024 
STAFF REPORT
FILE:
PUDC-24-124: Adiamo Subdivision Second Filing (Expedited Review)
REQUEST:
Vacate an alley and combine it with 5 Lots into 1 Lot.  
LOCATION:
Southeast of the intersection of W 19th St and O’Neil Avenue.
APPLICANT: 
AVI PC
OWNER:
Nineteenth and O’Neil LLC and Andiamo307 LLC
ZONING:
CBD Central Business District
PURPOSE: 
A plat creating one Lot for multifamily development. 
PREPARED BY:
Connor White, Planner II
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366

PUDC-24-124 
 
 
September 3, 2024 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the Andiamo Subdivision Second Filing Expedited Preliminary Plat, advancing the Final 
Plat to the Governing Body with staff recommended conditions. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
x 
UDC 2.1.2 Preliminary Plat Approval 
x 
UDC 2.1.3 Final Plat Approval  
x 
UDC Article 4 Subdivisions 
x 
UDC Article 5 Zoning Regulations 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting approval of a preliminary plat for roughly 1.05 acres of land. The proposed plat 
would combine 5 Lots into 1 Lot for multifamily development. A conditional use for Urban Loft development 
was approved by the Board of Adjustment on July 18, 2024.    
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Preliminary Plat, the Commission 
must make the following findings required in UDC 2.1.2.d  – a detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.2.d.1) 
 
On the future land use map found in PlanCheyenne, the subject land has been designated as 
Central Business District. The Central Business District (CBD) encompasses Downtown Cheyenne 
and functions as the community’s core business and civic area. Primary uses are intended for a 
variety of commercial businesses and civic uses. Business uses are generally smaller in scale and 
not dependent on direct vehicular access. Residential and office uses are encouraged on upper 
floors in the Downtown core, and as free-standing uses at the Downtown fringe.

PUDC-24-124 
 
 
September 3, 2024 
Foundation 1 of PlanCheyenne encourages community growth. This development is in response 
to market demands and is within the Urban Service Boundary. The development patterns 
proposed by this plat are compatible with the designations found on the future land use map. 
 
Foundation 7 of PlanCheyenne supports the use of existing infrastructure and timely provision 
of services (including emergency and education services). This development is proposed in an 
area which has largely existing infrastructure and limited expansion of infrastructure is 
necessary. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.  
 
2) The application reflects sound planning and urban design principles with respect to existing 
and potential future development in the vicinity, including consideration of current and future 
streets, open spaces, blocks and lots, and public utilities or facilities. (UDC 2.1.2.d.2) 
 
The proposed lot is to allow for multifamily development. This lot is on the fringe of the 
downtown and will take underutilized and vacant lots and create additional housing within 
walking distance of the downtown. The proposed lot size is similar to other lots that already 
exist within the area. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
3) The division of land meets the purposes, intent, and all applicable standards in Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.2.d.3) 
 
Community Facility Fees (4.2): 
For fees based on acreage, the area of the plat is pro-rated to the nearest tenth of an acre. 
Lands reserved as unbuildable (for right-of-way, detention, parks, remainder lots, etc), are 
exempt from fees. 
Land Acquisition Fee  
 
 
 
$385 ($350 per acre) 
Infrastructure Fee  
 
 
 
$385 ($350 per acre) 
Total Land Acquisition & Infrastructure Fees 
$770.00 
 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, the Land Acquisition & Infrastructure fees are payable at 
the time of the signing of the final plat.  
 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, Enhancement Fees are payable at the issuance of a 
building permit for each structure. 
Residential Enhancement Fee 
$400 per dwelling unit 
 
 
 
Transportation Network (4.3): 
This plat is occurring within an area of the community which has been previously developed. 
The current block layout and street network is well established and meets current code 
requirements. While this plat does propose vacating a portion of the existing alley, the 
vacation would not have an adverse effect on connectivity in the area. A letter from all 
utilities within the alley signing off on the vacation will be needed prior to approval.  
 
 
 
 

PUDC-24-124 
 
 
September 3, 2024 
Open Space (4.4): 
Open space is required 8% of the building footprint for lots 40,000 square feet or more. Due 
to the nature of the proposed development pattern, open space will be taken care of with 
future development actions. 
 
Required Improvements (4.5): 
This plat appears to be able to provide all required utilities to the lot within this 
development. Utilities exist within the adjacent rights-of-way. A letter from all utilities 
within the alley signing off on the vacation will be needed prior to approval. 
 
Public Safety Fees (4.6): 
Public Safety Fees are required at the time of construction of any residential building on the 
proposed lot. Per UDC Section 4.6.3.b, Public Safety Fees are payable at the issuance of a 
building permit for each structure.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.   
 
4) Any phasing proposed in the application is clearly indicated and demonstrates a logical and 
well-planned development pattern. This may include consideration of amenities and public 
facilities of the subdivision within each phase compared to the anticipated timing or the 
likelihood of future phases being constructed as planned. (UDC 2.1.2.d.4) 
 
No specific phasing is proposed with this project. 
 
Review criterion not applicable.   
 
5) The impacts identified in specific studies or technical reports that were prepared or requested 
to be prepared in association with the application, and whether the findings and 
recommendations of these reports, or any modifications or conditions on approval of the 
application, can mitigate these impacts. (UDC 2.1.2.d.5) 
 
Drainage: 
A drainage worksheet was provided. Engineering reviewed the worksheet and will require 
further information during the site plan.  
 
Traffic: 
A traffic worksheet was provided, and the Engineering Department has accepted what was 
provided.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
  
6) Lots proposed for development shall be buildable lots according to the existing or proposed 
zoning district standards, result in design and development patterns that meet the Intent and 
Applicability statements of the existing or proposed zoning district, and not aggravate 
nonconforming setbacks or currently adopted building and fire code access and separation 
requirements. (UDC 2.1.2.d.6) 
 
The proposed Lot is a buildable lot within the CBD zone. The CBD zone allows for 100% lot 
coverage and 0-foot setbacks to all lot lines.  
 

PUDC-24-124 
 
 
September 3, 2024 
The application complies with this review criterion.  
  
7) All parcels not proposed as buildable lots and which are not dedicated to and accepted by the 
City shall have a clearly defined entity responsible for ongoing maintenance.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.7) 
 
All Lots are proposed to be buildable Lots.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
8) The application will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development for 
adjacent property considering the existing zoning and Comprehensive Plan policies for that 
property.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.8) 
 
This plat will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.    
 
9) The application is not harmful to the public health, safety, and welfare. (UDC 2.1.2.d.9) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed plat would not adversely affect the public health, safety, 
or general welfare. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
10) Remainder lot(s) shall be clearly identified on the preliminary plat and shall not be developed 
or proposed for development for purposes.  Remainder lot(s) shall be noted and described on 
all plats either graphically or by legal description. (UDC 2.1.2.d.10) 
 
Remainder lots are not proposed with this plat. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
11) The layout and design of the final plat is in substantial compliance with the approved 
preliminary plat considering the number of lots or parcels, street and block layout, and access, 
and any deviations in the final plat brings the application in further compliance with the 
Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.3.d.1) 
 
The Final Plat submitted with the application is substantially similar to the Preliminary Plat 
submitted. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
12) The application meets any conditions or modifications of the approved preliminary plat. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.2) 
 
Through the review process and application of the Preliminary Plat, Staff recommends 
conditions be placed on the plat for prior approval by the governing body and prior to recording.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.   
   

PUDC-24-124 
 
 
September 3, 2024 
13) The application meets the purposes, intent, and applicable standards of Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.3) 
 
As a concurrent Preliminary and Final Plat application, this Final Plat review criterion is covered 
by the equivalent Preliminary Plat review criterion (subsection 3 above). 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, comments were received regarding the following issues: 
1. Minor clerical corrections. 
2. Utility usage of the alley proposed to be vacated. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff analysis is that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the expedited plat application as submitted by the applicant with conditions.   
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the expedited preliminary plat, forwarding the Final Plat to the 
Governing Body. 
2. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat with conditions and that a final plat addressing the 
conditions of approval be prepared and presented to the Governing Body.  
3. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat as submitted by the applicant and forward the 
preliminary plat to the Governing Body. 
4. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat subject to staff’s recommended conditions and 
forward the preliminary plat to the Governing Body.  
5. Recommend denial of the preliminary plat based on findings of denial. The Planning Commission 
must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the motion, 
findings for denial must be stated. 
6. Postpone the project until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
The Planning Commission may modify staff’s recommended conditions, delete conditions, add 
conditions, or decide contrary to staff’s recommendation. In the event conditions are modified, the 
conditions shall relate to applicable review criteria. In the event that the Commission acts contrary to 
staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the Commission shall cite specific reasons 
(based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
 
 
 

PUDC-24-124 
 
 
September 3, 2024

Staff recommends approval of the expedited Preliminary Plat, advancing the Final Plat to the Governing 
Body with the following condition: 
1. Prior to Governing Body approval, a letter with all utilities signing off on the vacation of the alley 
be provided.  
2. Prior to recording, have the land ownership and the owners listed on the face of the plat match.
