MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Connor White, Planner II 
Subject: PUDC-24-145 / 4614 E Pershing Blvd / 201 Agreement 
Date: 
September 9, 2024 
 
Background:  
The item for consideration is an exception to Cheyenne Water and Sewer District intergovernmental 
agreement (201 IGA) to implement the 1982 Wastewater Facilities Master Plan. This is approved by 
resolution.   
  
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral: 
September 9, 2024 
• 
Public Services Committee: 
September 17, 2024 
• 
Final Consideration: 
 
September 23, 2024 
 
Planning Commission Recommendation:  
 
The Planning Commission heard this item at their September 3, 2024, meeting and voted to recommend 
the Governing Body approve this exception.  
 
The Planning Commission Staff report is attached and has not been changed.    
 
Attachments: 
1. Resolution  
2. Planning Commission Staff Report 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION GRANTING AN EXCEPTION TO THE '201 
AGREEMENT' FOR THE OWNER OF THE PROPERTY LOCATED AT 
4614 EAST PERSHING BOULEVARD TO ALLOW INSTALLATION OF A 
SMALL WASTEWATER SYSTEM (SEPTIC SYSTEM)." 
 
WHEREAS, on April 25, 1983, the City of Cheyenne ("City"), the County of Laramie 
("County"), the City of Cheyenne Board of Public Utilities ("BOPU"), and the South Cheyenne 
Water and Sewer District ("District") entered into an agreement entitled "Memorandum of 
Agreement: Intergovernmental Contract Agreeing to Participate in the Implementation of the 
Findings of the 201 Facilities Plan Final Report for the City of Cheyenne, the South Cheyenne 
Water and Sewer District and Laramie County"; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the April 25, 1983 agreement is commonly referred to as the "201 
Agreement"; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the 201 Agreement defines the respective responsibilities and obligations of 
the signatory governmental entities with respect to the implementation of the "201 Facilities Plan" 
that was created in response to the requirements of Section 201 of the Federal Water Pollution 
Control Act; 
 
 
WHEREAS, one of the principal purposes of the 201 Agreement is to ensure that 
wastewater (sewage) generated in the areas immediately surrounding the corporate limits of the 
City of Cheyenne would preferentially be disposed of through the sanitary sewer systems operated 
by BOPU; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the 201 Agreement defines the "service area boundary" sometimes referred 
to as the "sewerable boundary" or "201 Area" as the area that "can be provided with Municipal 
Sewage Treatment Services via gravity flow"; 
 
 
WHEREAS, Applicant is requesting a 201 Exception as owner of a residential property 
which lies within the City of Cheyenne jurisdiction as well as within the 201 Area, specifically 
Parcel Identification Nos. 14662742101000 which is 0.35-acres in size; legally described as Lots 
33 & 34, Sunnyside Addition, 4th Filing, Resub TR 137-140; 
 
WHEREAS, the Applicant seeks to install a small wastewater system at a cost of 
approximately $14,500.00 rather than pay an estimated $316,650.00 cost to extend sanitary sewer 
service approximately 330 linear feet to the Applicant’s property; 
 
WHEREAS, pursuant to Section 3.4.(d) of the 201 Agreement, Laramie County 
government agreed to work with the City-County Health Unit to "ensure that no new permits for 
small wastewater treatment systems [septic systems] within the service area boundary that are 
inconsistent with the Facility Plan are issued"; 
 
 
1:25 pm, Sep 04 2024

WHEREAS, exceptions for the installation of small wastewater treatment systems (septic 
systems) may be granted pursuant to Section 4.1 of the 201 Agreement; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Applicants have applied for an exception pursuant to Section 4.1.(d) of 
the 201 Agreement which provides as follows:  
 
"4.1 Exceptions can be granted for the following reasons . . . (c) The exception will 
alleviate a clearly demonstrable hardship and compliance with the Facility Plan will 
exacerbate this hardship. A hardship will be determined based upon a 
cost/effectiveness study of sewer service provision to the property seeking the 
exception.; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, on September 3, 2024, the City Planning Commission recommended that the 
Governing Body grant the 201 Exception based on a finding that a demonstrable hardship exists 
under Section 4.1.(c) of the 201 Agreement to permit the installation of a small wastewater system 
on the subject property. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, the City of Cheyenne hereby consents to the granting of an 
exception pursuant to Section 4.1.(c) of the 201 Agreement to permit installation of a small 
wastewater system, as the exception will alleviate a clearly demonstrable hardship and compliance 
with the Facility Plan will exacerbate this hardship.; and 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the City of Cheyenne hereby consents to the granting 
of an exception pursuant to Section 4.1.(c) of the 201 Agreement to permit installation of a small 
wastewater system, subject to the following condition: (1) The property will be required to connect 
to sanitary sewer should it become readily available. 
 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2024. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor

SEPTEMBER 3, 2024 
STAFF REPORT  
 
 
FILE: 
PUDC-24-145: 4614 E Pershing Blvd - 201 Exception  
REQUEST: 
201 Exception to allow installation of a small wastewater system.  
LOCATION: 
East of US-30 and north of and adjacent to E Pershing Blvd. 
APPLICANT: 
James O’Connor 
OWNER: 
James O’Connor 
ZONING: 
County MU, Proposed to be MUR within the City 
CASE PLANNER: 
Connor White, Planner II 
 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PUDC-24-145 
 
 
September 3, 2024 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION(s): 
 
Move to recommend the City Council approve the exception to the 201 Facilities Plan requirement for 
City sewer service for a single-family home and permit installation of a small wastewater system, subject 
to Staff’s recommended conditions.  
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
Cheyenne Municipal Code Chapter 1.16 (Annexation and Infrastructure Improvements)  
• 
Cheyenne Municipal Code Chapter 13.12 (Sewer System) 
• 
201 Intergovernmental Agreement to participate in the implementation of the 201 Facilities 
Plan Final Report for the City of Cheyenne, the South Cheyenne Water and Sewer District, and 
Laramie County (1983) (IGA) 
• 
201 Facilities Plan Final Report (1982) 
• 
Plan Cheyenne 
 
The request for an exception must be submitted to the Planning Commission for review.  After 
Commission review, the exception would be approved or denied by the City Council. If the property 
seeking the exception is outside the Corporate Limits of the City and inside the 201 Service Area 
Boundary, then County Commissioner approval is also required. 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
In 1982 the City, County, and south Cheyenne Water and Sewer District executed an intergovernmental 
agreement to implement the 1982 Wastewater Facilities Master Plan. The plan and the IGA were 
intended to satisfy the Federal Clean Water Act requirements and to protect the safety and quality of 
the region’s groundwater from pollution, specifically from concentrated septic systems.   
 
The IGA requires connection to sewer systems for development of properties lying within the Urban 
Service Area identified in the Plan. The Facilities Plan defines the Urban Service Area as the area where 
“Municipal Sewerage Treatment services can be provided via gravity flow” [Section 1.0, Definitions of 
the IGA]. The IGA further states that the City of Cheyenne is to limit the use of on-site wastewater 
treatment systems within the Urban Service Area; this would include septic systems and package 
wastewater treatment plants. The IGA states that the City and County will not approve any development 
which is inconsistent with the Facility Plan unless granted an exception pursuant to Sections 4.0 to 4.7 of 
the IGA. 
 
The applicant applied for a permit to allow a small wastewater system. The permit was denied. After 
denial, the applicant was eligible to apply for an exception pursuant to the IGA. The request for an 
exception must be submitted to the Planning Commission for review. After Commission review, the 
exception would be approved or denied by the City Council and the County Commissioners if the 
property seeking the exception is outside the Corporate Limits of the City and inside the 201 Service 
Area Boundary. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 

PUDC-24-145 
 
 
September 3, 2024 
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING:

ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to approve the exception, the Commission must make the 
following findings per Sections 4.0 to 4.7 of the 201 IGA. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
Section 4.0: EXCEPTIONS FOR DEVELOPMENT THAT IS NOT CONSISTENT WITH THE PROPOSED FACILITY 
PLAN: 
 
4.1 Exceptions can be granted for the following reasons: 
(a) The proposed exception conforms with the criteria for exceptions in The City of Cheyenne 
Annexation Policies. 
(b) The exception can be shown not to be detrimental to the public health, safety and welfare [sic]. 
(c) The exception will alleviate a clearly demonstrable hardship and compliance with the Facility Plan 
will exacerbate this hardship. A hardship will be determined based upon a cost/effectiveness study of 
sewer service provision to the property seeking the exception. 
(d) The exception demonstrates substantial compliance with the proposed Facility Plan. 
 
Reason (a): This property is currently in the process of being annexed into City limits and does not 
have sanitary sewer readily available. Off-site sanitary sewer main extension would be required. The 
closest sewer main is at the intersection of Split Rail Court and Grasslands Parkway to the south. 
This proposal seeks an exception from tying into the sanitary sewer system for an existing single-
family home.   
 
Reason (b): The property owner has worked with Laramie County Health and a local contractor to 
design a small wastewater system that would meet all requirements and not be detrimental to the 
public health, safety and welfare.  
 
Reason (c): The property is already developed with a single-family home and the purpose of seeking 
the exception is to allow the continued usage of the property as a single-family home. No new 
construction or development of the parcel is proposed. The property does not have sanitary sewer 
readily available. The closest sewer main is at the intersection of Split Rail Court and Grasslands 
Parkway to the south. The applicant has provided a cost estimate to extend the sewer main 
compared to installing the proposed small wastewater system. The estimated cost to tie into the 
City sanitary sewer system would be approximately $316,650.00. This cost does not include the 
design, survey or system development fees to connect into the system. The cost to install the 

PUDC-24-145 
 
 
September 3, 2024 
 
proposed drain field would cost approximately $14,500.00. Extending the sewer main and tying into 
the City sewer system would be a hardship based upon a cost/effectiveness study.   
 
Reason (d): The facility would maintain the principal goal of the IGA – that is to prevent the 
environmental pollution of the land. The applicant has worked with Laramie County Health and a 
local contractor to design a new small wastewater system that would meet all requirements. 
 
4.2. Exceptions may only be considered if the proposal has been denied due to non-conformance with 
the proposed Facility Plan. 
 
The applicant applied for a permit to allow a small wastewater system on June 13, 2024.  The permit 
was denied on June 13, 2024. After denial, the applicant was eligible to apply for an exception 
pursuant to the IGA.  The exception request was filed with the Cheyenne-Laramie County Health 
Department on June 13, 2024 and forwarded to the City for review and consideration on August 13, 
2024. 
 
4.3. The request for exception must be submitted to the Planning Commission for review. 
 
This proposal is being submitted to the Planning Commission. 
 
4.4. After Planning Commission review, the exception will be approved or disapproved by: 
(a) The City Council if the property seeking exception is within the Corporate Limits; (b) The City 
Council and County Commissioners if the property seeking exception is outside the Corporate Limits 
and inside the Service Area Boundary. 
 
The property seeking an exception is currently being annexed into the City limits of the City of 
Cheyenne, and the exception will be submitted to the City Council after the Planning Commission 
review. 
 
4.5 (Due to a formatting error in the original Agreement, there is no Section 4.5) 
 
4.6. The replacement of an existing on-site wastewater treatment system on a property that is neither 
contiguous with the Cheyenne Corporate Limits nor within the DISTRICT's boundaries will not require 
an exception under this Agreement. 
 
This Section is not applicable to this proposal. 
 
4.7. The installation of a new on-site wastewater system on a single lot within the Service Area 
Boundary that is not contiguous with the Cheyenne Corporate Limits nor within the DISTRICT's 
boundaries will not require an exception under this Agreement, provided that the lot meets all the 
requirements of the Subdivision Regulations and any other requirements of this Agreement and was 
platted prior to the effective date of this Agreement. 
 
This Section is not applicable to this proposal. 
 
 
 
 
 
 

PUDC-24-145 
 
 
September 3, 2024 
 
RELEVANT PLANCHEYENNE POLICIES  
The following PlanCheyenne polices are related to this project: 
 
Foundation 1—Growing as a Community of Choice 
Policy 1.1.C: Regional Coordination on Land Use and Development Issues. 
 
 
Foundation 7—Developing in a Fiscally Responsible Way 
Principle 7.1: Our community will coordinate and plan for growth regionally to ensure efficient 
and cost-effective services and utilities. 
Principle 7.2: The Cheyenne Area will provide adequate public facilities and services for current 
and future residents in a fiscally responsible manner. 
 
The IGA was created to coordinate regionally on environmental health. The proposed small wastewater 
system provides a service commonly available to parcels that do not have readily available sewer. This is 
an existing single-family home that would need to extend the sewer infrastructure at their own cost 
which would not be cost effective as it would only serve their property. The exception request complies 
with the goals of PlanCheyenne.    
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff’s analysis is that the application meets the requirements to qualify for a 201 Exception with 
conditions. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend the Governing Body approve the exception as submitted by the applicant. 
2. Recommend the Governing Body approve the exception subject to Staff’s recommended 
conditions (Staff Recommendation). 
3. Recommend denial of  the exception based on findings of denial. The Planning Commission must 
conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the motion, findings 
for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the item until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
The Planning Commission may modify staff’s recommended conditions, delete conditions, add 
conditions, or decide contrary to Staff’s recommendation. In the event conditions are modified, the 
conditions shall relate to applicable review criteria. In the event that the Planning Commission acts 
contrary to staff’s recommendation, the Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review 
criteria) for a recommendation to approve or deny.   
 
STAFF’S RECOMMENDATION(s): 
 
Staff recommends approval of the exception for Lots 33 & 34, Sunnyside Addition, 4th Filing, Resub TR 
137 - 140 to allow a small wastewater system subject to the following condition: 
1. The property will be required to connect to sanitary sewer should it become readily available.  
 
 
 
 

PUDC-24-145 
 
 
September 3, 2024 
 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
 
1. Applicant Exception Request Letter 
2. Cheyenne-Laramie County Health Denial Letter 
3. Submitted Cheyenne-Laramie County Health Application  

Exception to the 201 Agreement 
James and Lydia O’Connor 
 
We come before you to request an exception to the “201 Facilities Memorandum of Agreement” 
between Chyenne City and Laramie County. Section 4 of the Memorandum of Agreement permits 
exceptions for a number of reasons. We request an exception on the following grounds: 
1) Section 4.1.(b) states: the proposed exception can be shown not to be detrimental to the public 
health, safety and welfare.  
 
We worked closely with Laramie County Environmental Health and a local contractor to devise 
a plan and design for a new leach field system that would meet all standards. 
 
2) Section 4.1.(c) states: the exception will alleviate a clearly demonstrable hardship and 
compliance with the Facility Plan will exacerbate this hardship. A hardship will be determined 
based upon a cost/effectiveness study of the sewer service provision to the property seeking 
exception. 
 
Connecting to the closest available sewer line would bankrupt our family. Cheyenne Board of 
Public Utilities calculated the cost to connect to the closet sewer line available to our property. 
As shown in the estimate attached, the cost to hook into city would be $316,650 in addition to 
the costs for design, surveying and construction. This amount is more than our property is 
worth and clearly puts our family in an extreme hardship. 
 
In contrast, the quote from Miller Industrial Services, LLC estimates that an improved septic 
system on our property would cost $14,500.  
 
The cost differential between $316,650 (plus costs for design, surveying and construction) and $14,500 
is extreme. It would be impossible for our family to financially afford connecting to city sewer. For 
these reasons, we respectfully request that our exception be approved.  
 
Thank you, 
 
James and Lydia O’Connor 
4614 E Pershing Blvd  
Cheyenne, WY 82001 
508-405-6109 
jaoconnorusa@gmail.com

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-09-03_TotalExtraction_10202757.pdf
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
The Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and City of Cheyenne Council Members 
FROM: 
Robin Lockman, City Treasurer 
DATE: 
September 12, 2024 
SUBJECT: 
Final Fiscal Year 2024 reappropriation to be considered for approval 
On September 23, 2024, the Governing Body will be referring a resolution to the Finance 
Committee to reappropriate funds for the Fiscal Year (FY) 2024 City of Cheyenne Budget per 
W.S. § 16-4-112 and 16-4-113.   
I am requesting an increase of $1,000,000 to both the revenue and expenditure budgets of the self-
insurance fund.  This request relates to last fiscal year.  We are still waiting for the incurred but 
not reported (IBNR) determination from Blue Cross Blue Shield (BCBS) of Wyoming.  IBNR is 
a term used by insurance companies and self-insured plans to describe the amount of money they 
may owe to claimants who have had a covered loss but have not yet filed a claim.  IBNR is an 
estimate because insurers don’t know how many losses have occurred or how severe each loss is.    
As this is an unknown amount, I have overestimated the amount of budget that may be required. 
For comparison, last year’s IBNR was $750,000.  Once we receive this estimate from BCBS in 
mid-October, I will post it to the general ledger and will finalize and disseminate the annual report 
of the City’s self-insurance fund. 
Please feel free to contact me at 637-6336 if you have questions.  

ENTITLED:  "A RESOLUTION RE-APPROPRIATING THE FISCAL YEAR 2024 BUDGET OF THE

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-09-03_TotalExtraction_54245110.pdf
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
The Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and City of Cheyenne Council Members 
FROM: 
Robin Lockman, City Treasurer 
DATE: 
September 12, 2024 
SUBJECT: 
Reappropriation to be considered for approval 
On September 23, 2024, the Governing Body will be referring a resolution to the Finance
Committee to reappropriate funds for the Fiscal Year (FY) 2025 City of Cheyenne Budget per 
W.S. § 16-4-112 and 16-4-113.  Below is more detailed information regarding this re-
appropriation request.  
ALL FUNDS - ENCUMBRANCES AND ROLLOVER ACCOUNTS (RESERVES) 
Each September my department prepares a large budget reappropriation request that includes 
carryover balances and encumbrances from the previous fiscal year for various City funds, 
including the One Percent Optional Tax Fund, Specific Purpose Tax Fund, and others.    
When the annual budget is approved each June, you may have noticed that numerous city funds 
are not included.  This is because funds available to carry over are not known until after the fiscal 
year ends.  Therefore, I have included carryover balances in this reappropriation request. 
I am also including General Fund line items that have unspent balances from FY 2024 to be carried 
over to FY 2025 for a total of $1,053,750.  This includes: 
•
$48,475 - City Council Ward funds
•
$25,750 - Police Department targeting system at the shooting range
•
$411,441 - Community Recreation and Events Capital Lottery Funds
•
$350,000 - Thomas Heights drainage improvement project
•
$58,084 - Parking lot and roadway surface repair at Lions Park
•
$160,000 - Cheyenne-Laramie County Public Health remodeling funds
Finally, I am including more detailed information on the purchase order (PO) encumbrances (see 
attached reports) from FY 2024 for a total of $1,196,572 in the General Fund, and $29,096,132 
in all other city funds.  Encumbrances included in this report are simply purchase orders that 
were created for goods and services ordered prior to, but not received until after, June 30, 2024.   

OTHER FUNDS - ADDITIONAL REVENUE/RESERVES 
In addition to carrying over numerous existing grants, the City of Cheyenne has received the 
following new grants and donations, by fund: 
Local and State Grant Fund (011) – grants received: 
• $2,000 – Lowes Hometown Grant for the Arboretum Head House Roof Replacement 
• $10,000 – Union Pacific Community Ties Giving Risk Reduction Grant 
• $100,000 – Innovative Finance Grant for Rob Roy Reservoir (partial reappropriation) 
• $100,000 – Old Sadie 15th Street Project Grant (partial reappropriation) 
Belvoir Recreation Fund (015) – grants received: 
• $25,000 – Discover Cheyenne Match Grant for the Belvoir Trail Project 
Law Enforcement Grant Fund (024) – grants received: 
• $5,000 – Enforcing Underage Drinking Laws (EUDL) Grant 
• $13,600 – Alcohol Compliance Grant 
• $13,600 – Tobacco Compliance Grant 
• $50,000 – Victim Assistance Program Services Contract (partial reappropriation) 
• $55,999 – Project Safe Neighborhood Grant 
Federal Grants Fund (025) – grants received: 
• $14,983 – Botanic Gardens Sensitive Species Germ Trials Grant (partial reappropriation) 
• $35,220 – Police Ballistic Sheilds Grant  
• $55,000 – Holliday Park/Lakeview Cemetery Reforestation Grant 
• $125,720 – Energy Efficiency and Conservation Block Grant (EECBG) for Civic Center 
Lighting Project 
• $200,000 – EPA Climate Pollution Reduction Grant (CPRG) (partial reappropriation) 
• $200,000 – Safe Streets for All Grant 
• $243,368 – Belvoir Ranch Trailhead Phase 1 Project Grant (partial reappropriation) 
• $1,000,000 – Pronghorn Crossing Project Grant (partial reappropriation) 
 
As always, if you have any questions, please give me a call at 637-6336 or email at 
rlockman@cheyennecity.org.

     That the Municipal Budget of the City of Cheyenne for Fiscal Year 2025 be re-appropriated as follows:

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-09-03_TotalExtraction_61763590.pdf
 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
 
TO: 
 
Honorable Mayor Patrick J. Collins, City of Cheyenne 
Members of the City Council, City of Cheyenne 
 
FROM: 
Erin Gates, PE, Staff Engineer  
 
DATE: 
September 18, 2024 
 
SUBJECT: 
A resolution authorizing hate City Engineer to execute Temporary Construction 
Easements on behalf of the City of Cheyenne. 
 
 
Please find attached a proposed resolution that provides authorization to the City Engineer for the 
execution of Temporary Construction Easements associated with the Storey Boulevard and 
Summit Drive Extension project and the East Dell Range Construction project. It is anticipated 
that both projects will require a number of Temporary Construction Easements will need to be 
secured to allow for the proper construction of the proposed improvements. It is anticipated that 
by extending the Mayor’s authorization to execute Temporary Construction Easements to the City 
Engineer for these two projects will more efficiently utilize the time of the Governing Body and 
city staff resources. Your support and approval of this resolution is respectfully requested. 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
CITY ENGINEER’S OFFICE 
2101 O’Neil Avenue  
Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION EXPRESSING THE SUPPORT OF THE GOVERNING 
BODY FOR THE CITY ENGINEER TO EXECUTE CERTAIN 
TEMPORARY CONSTRUCTION EASEMENTS ON BEHALF OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE ASSOCIATED WITH THE STOREY BOULEVARD 
AND SUMMIT DRIVE EXTENSION PROJECT AND THE EAST DELL 
RANGE CONSTRUCTION PROJECT." 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne routinely engages in public infrastructure projects to 
conduct necessary maintenance, repairs and improvements to the transportation elements of the 
city, including city streets, sidewalks and traffic control devices; 
 
WHEREAS, the scope of these projects varies but on many occasions the City Engineer's 
Office must coordinate with real property owners adjacent to the projects' bounds and secure 
Temporary Construction Easements (TCEs). These easements are necessary when the work of the 
City cannot be completed within the public right-of-way, for example when conducting certain 
earthwork or paving to ensure that the road surface follows the correct grade for proper drainage, 
safety and usability. 
 
WHEREAS, when a TCE is necessary as part of a city construction project, the governing 
body customarily affirms and supports the Mayor's authority to execute the attendant easement 
agreement; 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne is currently engaged in administering the Storey 
Boulevard and Summit Drive Extension Project, and the East Dell Range Construction Project, 
with both projects anticipated to require TCEs under a number of circumstances; 
 
WHEREAS, the objective of the Storey Boulevard and Summit Drive Extension Project is 
to reconstruct Summit Drive beginning at the intersection with North College Drive and continuing 
east to the intersection with Highland Road. The project would further improve and extend Storey 
Boulevard beginning at the intersection with Whitney Road and continue west to intersect with 
Highland Road. This project contemplates many improvements, not only to support the movement 
of traffic but to address stormwater drainage within the corridor; 
 
WHEREAS, the objective of the East Dell Range Construction Project is to reconstruct 
and expand Dell Range Boulevard to a five (5) lane urban section, beginning at the intersection 
with Griffith Avenue and continuing east to the intersection with Van Buren Avenue.  
 
WHEREAS, The East Dell Range Construction Project is planned to a include on-street 
bike lanes, a center lane to allow for vehicle access to businesses and adjoining streets and the 
installation of a traffic signal at the intersection with Van Buren Avenue. This signal is intended 
to support safer pedestrian crossings near Dildine Elementary School. This project also plans to 
incorporate improvements to the storm sewer, water, and sanitary sewer systems; 
 
11:07 am, Aug 19, 2024

Page 2 of 2 
WHEREAS, due to the large scope of the Storey Boulevard and Summit Drive Extension 
and the East Dell Range Construction Projects, the City Engineer's Office will need to secure 
numerous TCEs for each project and at different time intervals as each project progresses; 
 
WHEREAS, in the interest of efficiency and to better utilize the time of the Governing 
Body and city staff and resources, it is appropriate that the Mayor be able to execute the necessary 
TCEs associated with each City project without additional advisement or consent by the Governing 
Body; and 
 
WHEREAS, it is also appropriate that the Mayor be able to empower the City Engineer as 
his official designee to execute the necessary TCEs on behalf of the City in the interest of 
efficiency in the management of these projects. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the Mayor has the consent and support to execute all 
necessary temporary construction easements associated with the Storey Boulevard and Summit 
Drive Extension Project, and the East Dell Range Construction Project, without further 
consultation with the Governing Body; and  
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the Governing Body does not object to the Mayor 
delegating his signature authority for the execution of the temporary construction easements 
should he believe it would be appropriate and help expedite the completion of these projects. 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2024. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor  
 
 
(SEAL)  
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
____________________________________  
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 


--- END OF FILE ---


# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-09-03_TotalExtraction_50646355.pdf
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Seth Lloyd, Senior Planner 
Subject: PUDC-24-106 / Edgar Subdivision / Preliminary Plat Appeal 
Date: 
September 23, 2024 
 
Background:  
The item for consideration is a Preliminary Plat. This is typically approved by the Planning Commission 
and said approval is acknowledged by the City Council. In this case, the Planning Commission denied the 
Preliminary Plat outright. On September 12, 2024, the applicant requested in writing to appeal the 
Planning Commission’s decision. According to the Unified Development Code, an appeal of the Planning 
Commission’s decision goes to Council. As such, Council has the opportunity to uphold the Planning 
Commission's denial, or to overturn the denial.  
 
Staff is requesting one of two motions: 
- 
Move to uphold the denial 
- 
Move to overturn the denial 
Council may include any conditions they see fit on a motion to overturn to make the plat better meet 
review criteria. 
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral  
September 23, 2024 
• 
Public Services Committee  
October 8, 2024 
• 
Final consideration 
 
October 14, 2024 
 
Planning Commission Recommendation:  
The Planning Commission heard this item at their September 3, 2024 meeting and voted (by a vote  
of 4-1) to deny this application, due to failure to meet review criteria 2 and 8. 
 
The Planning Commission Staff report is attached.  
 
Attachments: 
1. Preliminary Plat 
2. Planning Commission Staff Report 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366

RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve the Edgar Subdivision Expedited Plat with staff 
exceptions and conditions. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.1.2 Preliminary Plat Approval 
• 
UDC 2.1.3 Final Plat Approval  
• 
UDC Article 4 Subdivisions 
• 
UDC Article 5 Zoning Regulations 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This site was originally designed specifically for drainage.  However, the drainage plan was reconfigured.  Due 
to these changes the property is now available for development.  The development requested is to final plat 
12 single-unit residential lots on this site.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Preliminary Plat, the Commission 
must make the following findings required in UDC 2.1.2.d  – a detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.2.d.1) 
 
This development is in conformance with the principles and future land uses identified in 
PlanCheyenne. The property is within the Urban Service Boundary and has access to all utilities. 
The future land use map within PlanCheyenne identifies this area as developing with u=Urban 
Residential development.  This is an infill opportunity which the comprehensive plan 
encourages. The comprehensive plan encourages development in existing stable neighborhoods 
to be compatible with the current development.  This proposal is consistent with current 
development patterns. 
  
The application complies with this review criterion.

PUDC-24-106 
 
 
September 3, 2024 
2) The application reflects sound planning and urban design principles with respect to existing 
and potential future development in the vicinity, including consideration of current and future 
streets, open spaces, blocks and lots, and public utilities or facilities. (UDC 2.1.2.d.2) 
 
The proposed plat divides the land into 12 lots that will develop 12 single family residential 
units.  Currently the property is accessible by Panorama Drive, and this access will not be 
changing.  These developments will match the exiting characteristics of the neighborhood being 
similar in size and use. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
3) The division of land meets the purposes, intent, and all applicable standards in Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.2.d.3) 
 
Community Facility Fees (4.2): 
For fees based on acreage, the area of the plat is pro-rated to the nearest tenth of an acre. 
Lands reserved as unbuildable (for right-of-way, detention, parks, remainder lots, etc), are 
exempt from fees. The acreage in the plat is 2.7 acres  
Land Acquisition Fee  
 
 
 
$945.00 
Infrastructure Fee   
 
 
 
$945.00  
Total Land Acquisition & Infrastructure Fees 
$1890.00 
 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, the Land Acquisition & Infrastructure fee are payable at 
the time of the signing of the final plat.  
 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, Enhancement Fees are payable at the issuance of a 
building permit for each structure. 
Residential Enhancement Fee 
$400 per dwelling unit 
 
 
 
 
Transportation Network (4.3): 
This plat is occurring within an area of the community that has been previously developed. 
There is limited opportunity to modify the street network.  The block this plat is occurring on 
is oversized however, due to topography and previous development there is no opportunity 
for this proposal to add a connection.  Staff recommends an exception to block size be 
granted.  
Open Space (4.4): 
Single family residential development under 10 acres is exempt from providing open space 
during the platting action. 
 
Required Improvements (4.5): 
Lot 41 may have issues getting water service depending on the location of the structure 
built on the lot. This plat otherwise appears to be able to provide all required utilities to the 
lots within this development. 
 
Public Safety Fees (4.6): 
Public Safety Fees are required at the time of construction of any commercial building or 
residential building on any of the proposed lots. Per UDC Section 4.6.3.b, Public Safety Fees 
are payable at the issuance of a building permit for each structure.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   

PUDC-24-106 
 
 
September 3, 2024 
4) Any phasing proposed in the application is clearly indicated and demonstrates a logical and 
well-planned development pattern. This may include consideration of amenities and public 
facilities of the subdivision within each phase compared to the anticipated timing or the 
likelihood of future phases being constructed as planned. (UDC 2.1.2.d.4) 
 
No specific phasing is proposed with this project. 
 
Review criterion not applicable.   
 
5) The impacts identified in specific studies or technical reports that were prepared or requested 
to be prepared in association with the application, and whether the findings and 
recommendations of these reports, or any modifications or conditions on approval of the 
application, can mitigate these impacts. (UDC 2.1.2.d.5) 
 
Drainage: 
Drainage was reviewed and all issues have been addressed. 
 
Traffic: 
A traffic study was submitted and reviewed. There were no concerns with the study provided. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
  
6) Lots proposed for development shall be buildable lots according to the existing or proposed 
zoning district standards, result in design and development patterns that meet the Intent and 
Applicability statements of the existing or proposed zoning district, and not aggravate 
nonconforming setbacks or currently adopted building and fire code access and separation 
requirements. (UDC 2.1.2.d.6) 
 
This plat is proposing 12 buildable lots into that we be developed into 12 single family homes or 
town houses. The lots will continue to be zoned MR for residential uses.  The proposed lot size 
would be a buildable lot for that zone district. Lot 35 appears to be between 38' and 39' wide at 
the front setback line. This would limit this lot to duplex development (and one neighboring lot).  
The plat contains significant topographical changes, which may make development on individual 
lots difficult.  However, it appears with the right engineering, these lots can be buildable.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
7) All parcels not proposed as buildable lots and which are not dedicated to and accepted by the 
City shall have a clearly defined entity responsible for ongoing maintenance.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.7) 
 
There are no unbuildable lots proposed in this plat.  There are changes in the topography across 
the property, but they are not significant enough to affect building. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
8) The application will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development for 
adjacent property considering the existing zoning and Comprehensive Plan policies for that 
property.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.8) 
 

PUDC-24-106 
 
 
September 3, 2024 
This plat will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development. The plat 
proposes dividing one lot into 12 smaller lots, with no major street connections, zoning or use 
changes, currently. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
9) The application is not harmful to the public health, safety, and welfare. (UDC 2.1.2.d.9) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed plat would otherwise not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare.  Due to the topography individual buildings will need to be 
carefully sited. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
10) Remainder lot(s) shall be clearly identified on the preliminary plat and shall not be developed 
or proposed for development for purposes.  Remainder lot(s) shall be noted and described on 
all plats either graphically or by legal description. (UDC 2.1.2.d.10) 
 
No remainder lots are proposed.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
11) The layout and design of the final plat is in substantial compliance with the approved 
preliminary plat considering the number of lots or parcels, street and block layout, and access, 
and any deviations in the final plat brings the application in further compliance with the 
Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.3.d.1) 
 
The Final Plat submitted with the application is substantially similar to the Preliminary Plat 
submitted. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
12) The application meets any conditions or modifications of the approved preliminary plat. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.2) 
 
Through the review process and application of the Preliminary Plat, Staff recommends minor 
corrections as conditions to the plat. These corrections should be resolved prior to the final plat 
being recorded.  The applicant is aware of the changes that are needed and will submit a final 
plan of the fixed issues before mailing. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.   
   
13) The application meets the purposes, intent, and applicable standards of Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.3) 
 
As a concurrent Preliminary and Final Plat application, this Final Plat review criterion is covered 
by the equivalent Preliminary Plat review criterion (subsection 3 above). 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.

September 3, 2024 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, comments were received regarding the following issues: 
1. Technical Corrections. 
2. Lot sizes and limiting development 
3. Drainage easement 
4. Block Size 
5. Sidewalk 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff analysis is that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the expedited plat application as submitted by the applicant with conditions. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend denial of the preliminary plat based on findings of denial. The Planning Commission 
must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the motion, 
findings for denial must be stated. 
2. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat subject to staff’s recommended conditions and 
forward the preliminary plat to the Governing Body.  
3. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat as submitted by the applicant and forward the 
preliminary plat to the Governing Body. 
4. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat with conditions and that a final plat addressing the 
conditions of approval be prepared and presented to the Governing Body.  
5. Recommend approval of the expedited preliminary plat, forwarding the Final Plat to the 
Governing Body. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
The Planning Commission may modify staff’s recommended conditions, delete conditions, add 
conditions, or decide contrary to staff’s recommendation. In the event conditions are modified, the 
conditions shall relate to applicable review criteria. In the event that the Commission acts contrary to 
staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the Commission shall cite specific reasons 
(based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the expedited Preliminary Plat, advancing the Final Plat to the Governing 
Body with the following exceptions and conditions: 
1. Exception: 
a. Exception to block size. 
2. Prior to recording: 
a. Add owners’ names to the notary.

Know all men by these presents that Edgar, Daniel S and Edgar, Jeanne M owner in fee simple of a portion Lot 9, Block 4, View Point
North 2nd Filing, located in the NE14SE14  of Section 22, T.14N., R.66W., 6th P.M., City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming, said
parcel more particularly described as follows: the following described property:
Commencing at City of Cheyenne Control Monument "Braehill", thence S64°20'23"E, 871.67 feet to a NE corner of said Lot 9, being a
found aluminum cap monumument and the POINT OF BEGINNING of this parcel description;  thence N88°20'10"E along the South
lines of Lots 20 and 14 of said Block 4 of said View Point North 2nd Filing, 134.96 feet to the SW corner of Lot 13 of said Block 4;
thence S45°16'42"E along the South lines of Lots 13, 12, 11 and 10 of said Block 4, 200.21 feet to an angle point on the South line of
Lot 10, said Block 4; thence continue along said South line the following two courses: N88°11'237”E, 84.80 feet; thence N61°56'39”E,
90.23 feet to intersect the Westerly right-of-way line of Panorama Drive;  thence along said Westerly right-of-way line on an arc of a
non-tangent curve to the right 287.53 feet, said curve having a radius of 170.16 feet, central angle of 96°48'58"and a chord bearing
S39°57'10"W, 254.52 feet; thence S88°21'39"W along said Northerly right-of-way line, 104.31 feet; thence departing said Northerly
right-of-way line, N18°30'37"W, 168.97 feet; thence S88°21'41"W, 71.94 feet; thence S1°54'13"E, 161.70 feet to a point on said
Northerly right-of-way line, thence S88°21'39"W, 266.44 feet to a point of curvature; thence continuing along said Northerly
right-of-way line on an arc of a tangent curve to the left 49.39 feet, said curve having a radius of 234.08 feet, central angle of
12°05'22"and a chord bearing S82°14'12"W, 49.30 feet to a point; thence departing said Northerly right-of-way line, N13°36'22"W,
145.48 feet to the SW corner of Lot 22 of said Block 4; thence N85°56'04"E along the South lines of Lots 22 and 21 of said Block 4,
300.80 feet to the SE corner of said Lot 21; thence N1°41'54"W along the West line of said Lot 21, 145.00 feet to the Point of
Beginning of this parcel description, containing 2.68 acres, more or less.
Has caused the same to be surveyed and platted into EDGAR SUBDIVISION, A REPLAT OF A PORTION OF LOT 9, BLOCK 4 VIEW POINT
NORTH 2ND FILING, does hereby declare the subdivision of said land as it appears on this plat to be thier free act and deed and in
accordance with their desires, and do hereby grant the easements for the purpposes indicated hereon.
Daniel S. Edgar
Jeanne M. Edgar

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-09-03_TotalExtraction_67175936.pdf
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Charles W. Bloom, AICP, Planning and Development Director  
Subject: Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne and the Downtown 
Development Authority 
Date: 
September 23, 2024 
 
The item for consideration is a Memorandum of Understanding between the City and the Downtown 
Development Authority (DDA).  
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral: September 23, 2024 
• 
Finance Committee: October 7, 2024 
• 
Final consideration: October 14, 2024 
 
The purpose of this MOU is to coordinate to the repair of sidewalks identified as trip hazards by the City 
Engineer’s office and to allow the City to receive reimbursement for all sidewalk repairs made by the City’s 
contractor. Reimbursement to the City will come from the Downtown Development Authority (DDA) and 
the property owner at a 50:50 ratio.  Pursuant to Cheyenne City Code 12.04.120.b the City will be 
responsible for any   curb and gutter repair associated with a sidewalk reconstruction. Responsibilities 
outlined in the MOU are summarized below. 
 
DDA Responsibilities: 
• 
Property owner notification on behalf of the City Engineer. 
• 
Collect payment from affected property owners in advance of work and relay payment to the City 
for services.  
• 
Coordinate with the City’s contractor the timing repairs to minimize business impact. 
• 
Coordinate the project with the City. 
 
City Responsibilities:  
• 
Follow its procurement policy to identify as contractor qualified to make sidewalk, curb, and gutter 
repairs. 
• 
Contract with a contractor to complete repairs to curbs, gutters, and sidewalks in problem areas. 
• 
Seek reimbursement from the DDA for the cost of the repairs made (Not to exceed the DDA’s 
$100,000 budget allocation).  
    
At the time of preparation of this memo, the DDA Board had not considered this MOU. The MOU will be 
reviewed by the DDA Board at their September 19, 2024, meeting. An update and any necessary 
substitute MOU will be provided at the September 23, 2024, Finance Committee meeting.  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

Memorandum of Understanding between 
the City of Cheyenne and the Downtown Development Authority 
1 
MEMORANDUM OF UNDERSTANDING 
BETWEEN 
THE CITY OF CHEYENNE AND THE DOWNTOWN DEVELOPMENT AUTHORITY 
 
 
This Memorandum of Understanding (MOU) is by and between the City of Cheyenne, a 
municipal corporation existing under the laws of the State of Wyoming, located at 2101 O’Neil 
Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001, hereinafter referred to as the “City”, and the Cheyenne 
Downtown Development Authority, a political subdivision organized and operating under the 
laws of the State of Wyoming, located 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001, 
hereinafter referred to as the “DDA.” The City and the DDA are individually or collectively 
referred to as “Party” or “Parties.” 
 
WHEREAS, within the DDA District, as defined by Section 2.76.040 of the Cheyenne 
City Code, several sections of sidewalk create trip hazards and are in need of repair; 
WHEREAS, property owners owe a general duty to maintain their property, including 
adjacent sidewalks, in a reasonably safe condition; 
WHEREAS, it is the legal responsibility of the property owner to maintain sidewalks 
adjacent to their property  under Sections 12.04.040 and 12.04.200 of the Cheyenne City Code; 
WHEREAS, Section 12.04.120(B) provides a special provision for the Downtown 
Development District properties relative to the repair or replacement of deteriorated curbs, 
gutters and sidewalks. This section provides in part that upon notification to a property owner by 
the City that the sidewalk abutting the owner’s property is in such a state of disrepair and/or 
noncompliance with current ADA standards that it poses a public safety hazard or an obstruction 
to pedestrian mobility, and that the repair or replacement of the sidewalk by the property owner 
is required, the property owner is not required to reconstruct or repair the abutting curb and 
gutter. This section further provides that curb and gutter repairs in the downtown development 
4:09 pm, Sep 18, 2024

Memorandum of Understanding between 
the City of Cheyenne and the Downtown Development Authority 
2 
district may be completed by the City at its expense upon the request of a property owner who 
has replaced the sidewalk abutting his or her property; 
WHEREAS, the DDA District is the heart of the community and the center of tourism for 
the City of Cheyenne and public safety is a primary concern for the DDA; 
WHEREAS, on March 11, 2024, the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne 
unanimously adopted Resolution No. 6391 supporting a sidewalk project in downtown 
Cheyenne. The intent of the Resolution was to create action plan to address deficiencies specific  
sidewalks in the downtown development district and to assist property owners in funding the 
necessary sidewalk repairs; 
 
WHEREAS, Resolution No. 6391 required that all City departments be responsible for 
identifying sidewalk problems within the DDA District and to coordinate on a program for the 
identification, notification, and citation of trip hazards on sidewalks; 
 
WHEREAS, on March 21, 2024, DDA staff provided Resolution No. 6391 to the DDA 
Board for consideration. Thereafter, the DDA Board created a subcommittee comprised of 
Janelle Rose, Danica Mrozinsky, Brian Bau, Scott Roybal and Pete Laybourn; 
 
WHEREAS, on April 8, 2024, DDA staff met with the subcommittee members Scott 
Roybal, Janelle Rose, Danica Mrozinsky, and Brian Bau to discuss program framework and 
funding levels;  
WHEREAS, the subcommittee recognized that this program should be implemented in 
coordination with the City Engineer’s implementation of the project and that the DDA’s role in 
the program would be complimentary to the City, based on a property owner reaching out to the 
DDA for utilization of the sidewalk grant program, and that the DDA would not be managing 
any aspects of the program;  

Memorandum of Understanding between 
the City of Cheyenne and the Downtown Development Authority 
3 
WHEREAS, the City Engineer’s office has reviewed ADA guidelines and has reviewed 
the recommendations of the 2014 Benchmark Engineering Report; 
WHEREAS,  the parties agree that the DDA shall manage the notification of, and 
payments by, property owners on behalf of the City Engineer, and that the City Engineer’s 
Office shall manage the sidewalk, curb, and gutter repairs under the program in accordance with 
established policies and procedures;  
WHEREAS, the City Engineer’s Office and the DDA have created a program to identify 
sidewalks in need of repair and remediation and in so doing, the parties have identified three (3) 
areas within the DDA District where trip hazards on sidewalks are significant; 
WHEREAS, some of the repairs may be interim in nature to minimize immediate safety 
hazards and additional measures may be required in the future; 
 
WHEREAS, the City will enter into agreements with one (1) or more a contractors, as 
deemed appropriate to repair sidewalks in areas identified by the City Engineer’s office; 
WHEREAS, the City’s miscellaneous concrete program funding has been considered to 
assist DDA property owners in funding curb and gutter repairs; 
 
WHEREAS, DDA staff have considered DDA Capital Improvement Grant (CIG) funding 
and have determined that that grant program is not an appropriate funding source. 
Notwithstanding, the DDA has designated up to one hundred thousand dollars ($100,000) in its 
budget to fund sidewalk repairs;  
 
WHEREAS, DDA staff will work with property owners to fund fifty (50%) of sidewalk 
repairs and will work with property owners to fund attendant curb and gutter repair ; and, 
 
WHEREAS, City departments will continue to coordinate with the DDA to identify any 
other sidewalk necessary repairs.  

Memorandum of Understanding between 
the City of Cheyenne and the Downtown Development Authority 
4 
NOW, THEREFORE, the Parties agree as follows: 
 
1. 
Purpose.  The purpose of this MOU is for the City and the DDA to coordinate for 
the repair of the sidewalks identified as trip hazards by the City Engineer’s Office, the necessary 
repair of curb and gutter associated with the sidewalk repairs, and to receive appropriate 
reimbursement for all sidewalk repairs made by the City’s contractor. 
 
2. 
Term.  This MOU shall commence on the date last executed by the duly 
authorized representative of each party to this MOU and shall remain in full force until 
terminated as provided herein. 
 
3. 
Payments.  The DDA will reimburse the City for its costs in completing the 
sidewalk repairs in the specified locations in an amount not to exceed one hundred thousand 
dollars ($100,000). 
 
4. 
Responsibilities of the Downtown Development Authority ("DDA").  The 
DDA shall: 
a. 
 Provide notice to identified property owners of the program and their 
fiscal responsibility if they elect not to participate in this program, as per Cheyenne City 
Code.   
b.   
Be responsible for securing payment from property owners for their 
portion of the cost of these repairs prior to commencement of work. 
c. 
Reimburse the City for the actual cost of sidewalk repairs made by any 
contractor under the program in a total amount not to exceed one hundred thousand 
dollars ($100,000). 

Memorandum of Understanding between 
the City of Cheyenne and the Downtown Development Authority 
5 
d. 
Coordinate with the City’s contractor regarding the timing of any sidewalk 
repairs so as to avoid any disruption to business operations if possible. 
e. 
Coordinate with the City regarding the project as a whole. 
 
5. 
Responsibilities of the City.  The City shall: 
a. 
Follow its procurement policy to identify contractors qualified to make 
sidewalk, curb, and gutter repairs under its applicable policies and procedures.  
b. 
Enter into a contract with any selected contractor for the repair of curb, 
gutter, and sidewalks in areas identified by the City Engineer’s office.  
c. 
Inspect and accept public improvement constructed under the provisions 
of this program. 
 
6. 
General Provisions.  
 
a. 
Amendments.  Any changes, modifications, revisions, or amendments to 
this MOU which are mutually agreed upon by the Parties to this MOU shall be 
incorporated by written instrument, executed, and signed by all Parties to this MOU. 
b. 
Assignment.  No Party may assign or transfer the responsibilities or 
agreements made herein without the prior written consent of the non-assigning Party, 
which approval shall not be unreasonably withheld. 
c. 
Applicable Law.  The construction, interpretation, and enforcement of this 
MOU shall be governed by the laws of the State of Wyoming. The courts of the State of 
Wyoming shall have jurisdiction over this MOU and the Parties, and the venue shall be in 
the First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming. 

Memorandum of Understanding between 
the City of Cheyenne and the Downtown Development Authority 
6 
d. 
Compliance with Laws.  The Parties shall keep informed of and comply 
with all applicable federal, state, and local laws and regulations in the performance of this 
MOU. 
 e. 
Indemnification.  The DDA agrees to indemnify, hold harmless, and 
defend the City from and against any and all liabilities, claims, penalties, forfeitures, and 
suits, and the cost and expenses incident thereto, including reasonable attorney’s fees, 
which may hereafter arise as a result the performance of the DDA’s duties, including 
death or bodily injury to any person, destruction or damage to any property, 
contamination of or adverse effects on the environment, or any violation of governmental 
laws, regulations, or orders to the extent caused by: (1) the DDA’s breach of any term or 
provision of this Agreement; or (2) any negligent or wrongful act, error, or omission by 
the DDA, or its employees, in the performance of this Agreement. The DDA 
acknowledges that it may incur a financial obligation to the City pursuant to the terms of 
this paragraph. 
f. 
Nondiscrimination.  The Parties shall comply with the Civil Rights Act of 
1964, the Wyoming Fair Employment Practices Act, Wyo. Stat. § 27-9-105, et seq., the 
Americans With Disabilities Act (ADA), as amended, 42 U.S.C. § 12101, et seq., and the 
Age Discrimination Act of 1975 and any properly promulgated rules and regulations 
thereto, and shall not discriminate against any individual on the grounds of age, sex, 
creed, color, race, religion, national origin, ancestry, pregnancy, or qualifying disability 
in connection with the performance under this MOU. 
g. 
Governmental Immunity.  The City does not waive governmental 
immunity by entering into this MOU and specifically retains all immunities and defenses 

Memorandum of Understanding between 
the City of Cheyenne and the Downtown Development Authority 
7 
available to it as a governmental entity pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et seq., and all 
other applicable laws. Designation of venue, choice of law, enforcement actions, and 
similar provisions should not be construed as a waiver of governmental immunity. 
h. 
Third-Party Beneficiary Rights.  The Parties do not intend to create in any 
other individual or entity, the status of third-party beneficiary and this MOU shall not be 
construed to create such status. The rights, duties, and obligations contained in this MOU 
shall operate only between the Parties to this MOU and shall inure solely to the benefit of 
the Parties to this MOU. The provisions of this MOU are intended only to assist the 
Parties in determining and performing their obligations under this MOU. The Parties to 
this MOU intend and expressly agree that only parties signatory to this MOU shall have 
any legal or equitable right to seek to enforce this MOU, to seek any remedy arising out 
of a Party’s performance or failure to perform any term or condition of this MOU, or to 
bring an action for the breach of this MOU. 
i. 
Force Majeure.  The performance of this MOU by the Parties shall be 
subject to force majeure including, but not limited to, acts of God, fire, flood, natural 
disaster, war or threat of war, acts or threats of terrorism, civil disorder, unauthorized 
strikes, governmental regulation or advisory, recognized health threats as determined by 
the World Health Organization, the Centers for Disease Control, or local government 
authority or health agencies (including, but not limited to, the health threats of COVID-
19, H1N1, or similar infectious diseases), curtailment of transportation facilities, or other 
similar occurrence beyond the control of the Parties, where any of those factors, 
circumstances, situations, or conditions or similar ones prevent, dissuade, or 
unreasonably delay the performance required by this MOU. This MOU may be cancelled 

Memorandum of Understanding between 
the City of Cheyenne and the Downtown Development Authority 
8 
by any Party, without liability, damages, fees, or penalty, and any unused deposits or 
amounts paid shall be refunded, for any one or more of the above reasons, by written 
notice to the other Party. 
j. 
Severability.  If any provision of this MOU is found to be invalid or 
unenforceable for any reason, the remaining provisions will continue to be valid and 
enforceable. If a court finds that any provision of this MOU is invalid or unenforceable, 
but that by limiting such provision it would become valid and enforceable, then such 
provision will be deemed to be written, construed, and enforced as so limited. 
k. 
Notice.  All notices arising out of or from the provisions of this MOU 
shall be in writing and given to the Party either by regular mail or delivery in person. 
l. 
Termination.  Any Party may terminate its participation in this MOU, with 
or without cause, by providing thirty (30) days written notice to the other Party. 
m. 
Prior Approval.  This MOU shall not be binding upon the Parties, no 
services shall be performed under the terms of this MOU, and no payments shall be made 
until this MOU has been reduced to writing and approved by all necessary authorities. 
n. 
Entirety of Contract.  This MOU represents the entire and integrated 
agreement between the Parties and supersedes all prior negotiations, representations, and 
agreements, whether written or oral. 
 
[SIGNATURES ON FOLLOWING PAGE] 

Memorandum of Understanding between 
the City of Cheyenne and the Downtown Development Authority 
9 
7. 
Signatures.  In witness whereof, the Parties to this MOU, through their 
duly authorized representatives, have executed this MOU on the days and dates set out 
below and certify that they have read, understood, and knowingly and voluntarily agreed 
to the terms and conditions of this MOU.

MEMO 
 
TO: 
 
Honorable Mayor Patrick J. Collins, City of Cheyenne 
Members of the City Council, City of Cheyenne 
 
FROM: 
Doug Klahn, Construction Manager  
 
DATE: 
September 2, 2024 
 
SUBJECT: 
Contract Modification #1 to Asphalt Preservation LLC. for the 2024 Surface Seal 
#1 project.   
 
 
Please find the attached proposed contract modification for the above referenced city project.   
Construction and Engineering Staff have discussed the additional work required to complete this 
project and determined that the costs associated with the supply and installation of this material is 
in accordance with the original contract unit pricing. Additionally, this contract modification is 
beneficial to the city as it requires no additional mobilization costs and ensures that this project is 
completed to the city’s specifications. The additional cost associated with this work is estimated 
at $193,638.42. In addition, this will require an extension of the completion date to November 15, 
2024. Staff respectfully request your approval of this contract modification.

September 2, 2024 
 
 
The above referenced contract is amended or modified as set forth in the following and these 
amendments and modifications of the plans and specifications are agreed to by the parties. All other 
aspects of the original agreement remain unchanged and in full force and effect. 
 
I. Description of Changes 
 
A change in aggregate material was made due to lack of a local source. This change created the 
need for additional aggregate, emulsion, and trucking necessary to complete this project 
properly.

Not Applicable.

The contract completion date will be adjusted to November 15, 2024.

THIS AGREEMENT, entered into this ____ day of ___________, 2024, by and between 
the CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, hereinafter referred to as the “CITY”, and S&S 
Builders LLC, hereinafter referred to as the “CONTRACTOR”. 
 
WITNESSETH that the Contractor and the City, for the considerations stated herein, 
mutually agree as follows: 
 
ARTICLE 1.  STATEMENT OF WORK.  The Contractor shall furnish all supervision, 
technical personnel, labor, materials, machinery, tools, equipment, and services, including utility 
and transportation services, and perform and complete all work in an efficient and workmanlike 
manner in the construction of the "Dutcher Athletic Complex Restroom and Concession Building" 
project all in strict accordance with the Contract Documents including all addenda thereto, 
numbered and dated: Addendum One (1), dated August 27, 2024, and Addendum Two (2), 
dated August 29, 2024. 
 
ARTICLE 2.  RESPONSIBLE DESIGNEE FOR THE CITY.  The Contractor shall in any 
and all matters relating to the scope of services to be provided under this Contract or any other 
provisions herein, contact the City Engineer, or his/her designated representative. 
 
ARTICLE 3.  TIME FRAME FOR COMPLETION.  The services to be performed under 
this Agreement shall commence on the date stipulated in the “Notice to Proceed” that will be issued 
by the City.  The work shall be completed by April 1, 2025.  If the work has not been completed 
within the time stipulated above, including any extensions of time issued by the City for excusable 
delays, the Contractor and his/her sureties shall pay the City fixed, agreed liquidated damages, as 
stipulated in the Supplemental Conditions, for each calendar day of delay until the work is 
completed. 
 
ARTICLE 4.  COMPENSATION AND METHOD OF PAYMENT.  The CITY will pay 
the CONTRACTOR for the performance of the Contract in current funds, in an amount of Six 
Hundred Eighty-Seven Thousand, Eight Hundred Eight Dollars ($687,808.00). In the event 
there are changes in the estimated quantities shown on the Bid Proposal, the unit prices multiplied 
by the actual quantities shall govern, and the total contract amount will be adjusted accordingly.  
The City agrees to pay the above amount for contractual services in the following manner, upon 
receipt of appropriate documentation: 
 
a. 
The Contractor will be paid on a monthly basis for the percentage of 
estimated work completed.  Submittal will be at least seven (7) business days prior to the 
payable due date as established annually by the City Treasurer’s Office.  The pay request 
shall be submitted on the Contract Payment Request Form and Itemized Pay Request or 
5:59 pm, Sep 03 2024

Page 2 of 2 
the AIA Documents G702 and G703.  The engineer will review the estimate for approval 
prior to payment. 
b. 
The City will withhold five percent (5%) of the dollar value of the work 
completed for a minimum of forty-one (41) calendar days after Notice of Final Settlement 
has been published in accordance with Wyo. Stat. §16-6-116.  Upon completion of the 
work under this Contract, the Contractor shall submit a Contractor’s Certificate of 
Completion; the Consent of Surety; Final Waivers of Lien from the Contractor, and all 
Sub-Contractors, Suppliers and Materialmen; Affidavit of Release of Liens; Affidavit of 
Payment; and a current Workers Compensation Certificate.  Final payment will not be 
made until the above documents have been received by the City and all items on the Punch 
List have been completed, and the advertising requirements have been met. 
 
ARTICLE 5.  CONTRACT.  The executed Contract Documents shall consist of the 
following:

2. Addenda;

5. Signed Bid Proposal; 
 
6. General Conditions and Insurance;

8. Part IV Forms & Notices;

Provisions; and

MEMORANDUM

FROM:

DATE:

SUBJECT: 
Consideration of Bid #S-5-25 for an Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and S 
& S Builders, LLC for the Pronghorn Crossing Site Infrastructure project 
______________________________________________ 
Bid #S-5-25 was solicited for the Pronghorn Crossing Site Infrastructure project (Civil Site Plan Map 
Attached). 
 
Two (2) bidders responded to this bid, and the bid tabulation is attached.  The Bids were received 
from: 
1. S & S Builders LLC 
2. Reiman Corp. 
 
The bids were reviewed by Doug Klahn (1% Construction), Erin Gates (Engineering), and Lovas 
Engineering (Project Architect) and their recommendation is to accept the lowest responsive bid from 
S & S Builders LLC. 
 
The total Bid Award is $2,039,808.00. 
 
Project Completion is scheduled for October 31, 2026.

$1,742,318.00

(Wyoming ARPA funds through SLIB - Affordable Housing Funds)

CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING 
AGREEMENT 
 
Bid No. S-5-25 
 
Contract No. _______ 
 
THIS AGREEMENT, entered into this ____ day of ___________, 2024, by and between 
the CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, hereinafter referred to as the “CITY”, and S & S 
Builders, LLC, hereinafter referred to as the “CONTRACTOR”. 
 
WITNESSETH that the Contractor and the City, for the considerations stated herein, 
mutually agree as follows: 
 
ARTICLE 1.  STATEMENT OF WORK.  The Contractor shall furnish all supervision, 
technical personnel, labor, materials, machinery, tools, equipment, and services, including utility 
and transportation services, and perform and complete all work in an efficient and workmanlike 
manner in the construction of the "Pronghorn Crossing Site Infrastructure" project all in strict 
accordance with the Contract Documents including all addenda thereto, numbered and dated: 
Addendum One (1), dated September 12, 2024, Addendum Two (2), dated September 12, 
2024, and Addendum Three (3), dated September 16, 2024. 
 
ARTICLE 2.  RESPONSIBLE DESIGNEE FOR THE CITY.  The Contractor shall in any 
and all matters relating to the scope of services to be provided under this Contract or any other 
provisions herein, contact the City Engineer, or his/her designated representative. 
 
ARTICLE 3.  TIME FRAME FOR COMPLETION.  The services to be performed under 
this Agreement shall commence on the date stipulated in the “Notice to Proceed” that will be issued 
by the City.  The work shall be completed by October 31, 2026.  If the work has not been 
completed within the time stipulated above, including any extensions of time issued by the City 
for excusable delays, the Contractor and his/her sureties shall pay the City fixed, agreed liquidated 
damages, as stipulated in the Supplemental Conditions, for each calendar day of delay until the 
work is completed. 
 
ARTICLE 4.  COMPENSATION AND METHOD OF PAYMENT.  The CITY will pay 
the CONTRACTOR for the performance of the Contract in current funds, in an amount of Two 
Million, Thirty-Nine Thousand, Eight Hundred Eight Dollars ($2,039,808.00). In the event 
there are changes in the estimated quantities shown on the Bid Proposal, the unit prices multiplied 
by the actual quantities shall govern, and the total contract amount will be adjusted accordingly.  
The City agrees to pay the above amount for contractual services in the following manner, upon 
receipt of appropriate documentation: 
 
a. 
The Contractor will be paid on a monthly basis for the percentage of 
estimated work completed.  Submittal will be at least seven (7) business days prior to the 
payable due date as established annually by the City Treasurer’s Office.  The pay request 
shall be submitted on the Contract Payment Request Form and Itemized Pay Request or 
3:23 pm, Sep 17 2024

Page 2 of 2 
the AIA Documents G702 and G703.  The engineer will review the estimate for approval 
prior to payment. 
b. 
The City will withhold five percent (5%) of the dollar value of the work 
completed for a minimum of forty-one (41) calendar days after Notice of Final Settlement 
has been published in accordance with Wyo. Stat. §16-6-116.  Upon completion of the 
work under this Contract, the Contractor shall submit a Contractor’s Certificate of 
Completion; the Consent of Surety; Final Waivers of Lien from the Contractor, and all 
Sub-Contractors, Suppliers and Materialmen; Affidavit of Release of Liens; Affidavit of 
Payment; and a current Workers Compensation Certificate.  Final payment will not be 
made until the above documents have been received by the City and all items on the Punch 
List have been completed, and the advertising requirements have been met. 
 
ARTICLE 5.  CONTRACT.  The executed Contract Documents shall consist of the 
following: 
 
1. This Agreement;  
2. Addenda; 
3. Invitation for Bids; 
4. Instructions to Bidders; 
5. Signed Bid Proposal 
6. Supplemental Federal Provisions & 
Clauses; 
 
7. General Conditions and Insurance; 
8. Supplemental Conditions; 
9. Part IV Forms & Notices; 
10. Specifications and Special 
Provisions; and  
11. Drawings. 
 
This Agreement, together with other documents enumerated in this Article 5, which said 
other documents are as fully a part of the Contract as if hereto attached or herein repeated, forms 
the Contract between the parties hereto. 
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, THAT the governing body of the City of Cheyenne has 
authorized the Mayor as Executive Officer of the City to enter into this Agreement, and that the 
parties hereto have caused this Agreement to be executed on the day and year in the first part herein 
written.
